# Verification log

Entries were run against the dirty Kotlin worktree on the dates shown below. A passing focused task verifies only the stated module and target. The full platform gate remains open.

| Check | Result | Scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Unicode code points in custom string masks | On 2026-11-24, a pure core regression first failed because one supplementary character consumed two custom-token slots and dropped the following letter. The parser now consumes whole code points and maps the displayed caret back to UTF-16 offsets. Four core mask cases pass on JVM and Wasm browser; 16 mounted Material/Cupertino mask cases pass on JVM, JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator, including raw/formatted storage and `maxLength: 2` with an emoji plus letter. The seven-module JVM gate passes 608/21/90/6/547/20/2,665 tests with zero failures/errors; full demo JS/Wasm browser suites pass 2,648/2,647 tests with zero failures/errors/skips. Android debug APK and exact three Pages distributions build. The first Wasm demo attempt stalled after entering Chrome; its clean retry completed. | This is a local green commit checkpoint. Physical-device IME composition, clipboard paste, custom-regex parity and accessibility remain open. |
| Enum and oneOf selector placeholders | On 2026-11-24, eight mounted Material/Cupertino cases cover closed enum, oneOf and searchable selectors, live authored hint changes without data writes, explicit empty and nonstring hints, and a stored empty choice. All eight pass on JVM, JS Chrome, Wasm Chrome and the iOS simulator. The seven-module JVM gate passes 607/21/90/6/547/20/2,663 cases with zero failures/errors. A clean full JS Chrome run passes 2,646 tests with zero failures/errors/skips; the full Wasm browser task, Android debug APK and exact three Pages distributions also succeed. Two earlier JS attempts stopped in Karma reporting; clearing generated JS test XML and rerunning produced the complete result. | This is a local green commit checkpoint for selector placeholders. Physical-device accessibility and remaining hint-bearing controls remain open. |
| Ordinary text placeholder update | On 2026-11-24, paired Material/Cupertino mounted tests confirm that changing only a static placeholder preserves the focused editor, caret position, stored text and write count. The seven-module JVM gate passes 607/21/90/6/547/20/2,655 tests with zero failures or errors. Ledger JSON and `git diff --check` pass. | JVM evidence for ordinary text fields. Non-JVM runtime behavior and other hint-bearing controls remain open; generic `$dynamic` overlays are experimental in the pinned spec. |
| Table dialog viewport reflow and body scroll | On 2026-11-23, the first browser assertion incorrectly accepted zero-sized accessibility boxes as visible. Correcting it reproduced a clipped Apply/Cancel footer after a long automatic-height row editor shrank from 800×600 to 360×260. The shared frame now gives the body the height left after its title and footer. The focused `ArrayRowDetailGeometryUiTest` JVM class passes, and four rebuilt production Chrome cases pass for Material/Cupertino row and composite cell dialogs. They confirm visible title and action bounds, an initially visible first field scrolling out, a later field scrolling into the body, fixed title/footer, unchanged page scroll and no data write. The seven-module JVM gate passes 605/21/89/6/547/20/2,613 tests with zero failures/errors (13 existing core skips); Android debug APK, JS/Wasm demo distributions, Web Component production bundle and Material iOS/Wasm/Android compilations pass. This is a local green commit checkpoint. | Browser evidence for §18.21 dialog re-clamping, fixed header/footer and inner body scrolling. Direct screen-reader behavior and native device viewport changes remain open. |

Focused core/picker tests pass on JVM, JS browser and Wasm browser; full core and extended-renderers iOS simulator suites pass. The validator library emits a duplicate tail `items` error when a 2020-12 prefix value fails its own `prefixItems` schema. The adapter removes that duplicate when a matching prefix failure is present, preserving actual tail errors; focused JVM tests cover inline and referenced schemas. Broader modern-dialect combinations and non-JVM execution remain open.

The Compose UI harness now executes on JVM and JS browser. Android device, iOS and Wasm mounted interactions, screenshots and device accessibility checks still need their own executed evidence. Full builds and tests are reserved for the planned gates or broad integration risk.

## Array choices: explicit multi-select (2026-11-11)

The shared finite-string profile and independent Material/Cupertino multi-select renderers now select above automatic enum-array checkboxes. They preserve unknown imported array entries, store values separately from displayed labels, enforce unique selection and restricted min/max counts, support localized search, and repair a wrong-shaped imported value only after an explicit choice. Twelve mounted JVM cases pass across both families; four pure core cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator. The affected full JVM suites and iOS/JS/Wasm compilation passed before the final wrong-shaped-value repair, and focused JVM cases passed afterward. Renderer inventory, spec candidate and example checks, JSON syntax, and `git diff --check` pass.

The broad `:core:jsBrowserTest` run reported two failures outside the new array-choice suite: `LayoutSizingTest` float precision and `TableLabelSummaryTest` regular-expression syntax. Its four `ArrayChoiceProfileTest` cases passed. Mounted browser/device interaction, the imported fixture workflow and chips remain unverified.

## Array choices: explicit chips (2026-11-11)

The shared profile now distinguishes finite string choices from free string entry for `variant: "chips"`. Material and Cupertino use position-addressed removal, preserve duplicates when `uniqueItems` is absent, keep free text local until Add, and enforce min/max item counts when restricted. Six mounted JVM cases pass across both families; six pure core cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Core plus both renderer families compile for iOS simulator, JS and Wasm. The renderer registry checker reports 37 entries in each family. Browser/device interaction, imported fixture, readonly and accessibility checks remain open.

The full affected JVM suites (`:core:jvmTest`, `:material-renderers:jvmTest`, `:cupertino-renderers:jvmTest`, `:examples-compose:jvmTest`) pass after chips integration. Two additional Material/Cupertino mounted JVM tests navigate the imported `array-choices` fixture, verify a titled choice, remove only one of the duplicate scan tokens, and remove an invalid imported choice. This establishes partial fixture coverage; the complete workflow and non-JVM interaction remain open.

Four further chips JVM cases pass: readonly free entry exposes no text-edit action and disables Add/Remove in both families; finite-choice search filters displayed titles instead of stored constants. The chips suite now has 10 passing mounted cases. The first readonly assertion expected a disabled field to retain `SetText` semantics; the corrected assertion verifies the action is absent.

## Array choices: live locale and positional names (2026-11-11)

The imported fixture translates constant-based `oneOf` choices under stored-value keys such as `teams.ops`; the shared oneOf mapper used title-based keys. Material and Cupertino array-choice renderers now try the stored-value key with the translated title as fallback. Four new mounted JVM cases keep an open chips/multi-select search in both families while switching to Bulgarian: the English query stops matching, a Bulgarian query finds the translated label, and selection still stores `ops`. Repeated chip removal buttons now expose distinct positional accessibility names, verified by paired mounted tests. Cupertino free-token and search fields have explicit accessible names. Selection accessibility and device/browser interaction remain open.

The affected full JVM suites pass after the locale/accessibility edits. Material and Cupertino also compile for iOS simulator, JS and Wasm after those edits; only existing Cupertino experimental-API warnings remain. The chips mounted suite now has 14 passing cases.

## Array choices: equal-label selection names (2026-11-11)

Both finite-choice menus now expose a stored-value suffix to accessibility when translated labels collide, while keeping the visible label unchanged. Paired Material/Cupertino chips and multi-select JVM tests select `Team (b)`/`Team (a)` and `Team (fin)`/`Team (ops)` through those names and verify the distinct stored JSON values. Other target screen-reader behavior remains unverified.

Both array-choice families now give Add actions a field-specific accessibility name. Paired imported `array-choices` JVM tests use those names to exercise all three categories: automatic checkbox selection; alert and team multi-select; free tag commit, duplicate scan removal and finite labels; bounded office choices and invalid imported preference repair. The checkbox case uses the checkbox semantics action. Browser/device interaction remains unverified.

The affected full JVM suites pass after these accessibility and fixture edits. Material and Cupertino compile for iOS simulator, JS and Wasm; only existing Cupertino experimental-API warnings remain. Renderer-registry, spec coverage/candidate and snapshot checks, ledger JSON syntax, and `git diff --check` pass.

## Core KMP browser execution gate (2026-11-11)

The previous full JS browser run had two unrelated failures: a table-summary residual-placeholder regex used an unescaped closing brace, which JS Unicode regex rejects, and a layout-width test required exact Float equality despite platform rounding. The regex now escapes both braces, and the numeric assertion uses a 0.001 logical-unit tolerance. Full core suites now pass on JVM (398 tests, 13 skipped), JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator (397 tests each, 13 skipped), with no failures or errors. These execute the six pure array-choice profile cases on each target; mounted renderer browser/device interaction remains unverified.

The `:examples-compose:compileTestKotlinJs` task passes, but its full `:examples-compose:jsBrowserTest` run completes 608 tests with 579 failures. All 579 report the same `org_jetbrains_skia_Surface__1nMakeRasterN32Premul is not a function` error while constructing `SkikoComposeUiTest`, before renderer composition. The array-choice mounted tests therefore have no JS interaction evidence from that runner. Browser UI verification needs a working Skiko test bootstrap or an integration harness against the webcomponent; compiling the tests alone is not interaction evidence.

## Mounted JS browser runner (2026-11-11)

The generated Skiko WASM exports `Surface__1nMakeRasterN32Premul`; its JS wrapper was called before the asynchronous WASM initialization completed. A JS-only Karma root hook now waits until Skiko's GL proxy is usable before any mounted test starts. Two full `:examples-compose:jsBrowserTest` runs pass all 608 tests with no failures or skips, including the 14 chips cases, 12 multi-select cases and two imported array-choices fixture cases. The hook is scoped to `build/js` and is not loaded by Wasm tests.

`:examples-compose:wasmJsBrowserTest` did not reach execution: its test executable compilation stopped with `No space left on device`. Only generated Wasm caches were removed, restoring about 1 GiB of workspace free space. The Wasm mounted interaction gate remains unverified; the earlier pure core Wasm browser suite passed.

## Locale and mounted target checkpoint (2026-11-11)

The shipped renderer catalogs, demo selector and imported example translations now include English and Bulgarian only. Browser-language selection maps Bulgarian tags to `bg` and all other tags to `en`; a common test covers `bg-BG`, `en-US` and unsupported `de-DE`. The affected core (398), examples (12), Material (51), extended (309), and examples-compose (609) JVM suites pass. Core JS and Wasm browser suites pass 397 tests each; examples-compose JS and Wasm browser suites pass 609 mounted tests each. `scripts/check-spec-coverage.mjs` reports 49 classified examples, 42 imported and seven explicit gaps/exclusions. These supersede the earlier JS runner and Wasm disk-space results above.

The iOS simulator mounted suite initially ran 608 tests with four failures: two Cupertino demo cases required a `UIViewController` absent from `runComposeUiTest`, and two row-detail geometry cases did not restore focus to the opener after Close. The demo test now uses a neutral Material theme wrapper while still selecting the Cupertino renderer registry. The shared row-detail action explicitly returns focus after Close and Apply; the geometry test asserts both paths. `:examples-compose:iosSimulatorArm64Test` now passes 609 mounted tests with no failures. The affected JVM suite and both mounted browser suites also pass 609 tests each after the focus change. Android device interaction remains unverified.

## Shared array action policy (2026-11-11)

The §18.12 resolver reads `disableAdd` and `disableRemove` from element options over top-level host config, treating explicit false as an override and defaulting to false. Table, expandable, ListWithDetail, Material/Cupertino tuple, automatic checkbox, chips, multi-select and file-array renderers now consume it. Collection actions guard handlers and keep reorder available. File-array picker callbacks are rejected after Add permission is revoked; pending validity returns to Valid without changing committed data.

`ArrayActionPolicyTest` passes three pure JVM cases. Focused mounted JVM suites pass twelve `ArrayActionPolicyUiTest`, eight `ArrayChoiceActionPolicyUiTest`, and 66 `FileArrayControlUiTest` cases, including four new file policy cases. The broad affected run passes core JVM 401 tests, core iOS/JS/Wasm 400 each, Material JVM 51, Cupertino JVM 4, extended JVM 313, and examples-compose JVM/iOS/JS/Wasm 629 each, with zero failures. `:examples-compose:compileAndroidMain` and `:extended-renderers:compileAndroidMain` pass. Android device interaction and real file-picker behavior remain unverified.

## Extended renderer target verification (2026-11-11)

The full extended-renderer browser runs exposed three target-specific issues: the JS mounted-test runner started before Skiko WASM was ready, a shared label-expression regex was invalid under JavaScript Unicode regex rules, and malformed base64 image URLs did not reliably produce a Coil load error on Wasm. The extended JS runner now waits for Skiko readiness; the regex uses escaped closing delimiters; and malformed base64 image data enters the existing load-failure and retry path deterministically. The JS/Wasm `_self` link opener uses the supplied URI handler, allowing host interception while `_blank` retains DOM anchor behavior.

Full extended-renderer suites pass: JVM 315, iOS simulator 309, JS browser 309, and Wasm browser 309 tests, all with zero failures. Android main compilation passes. The shipped locale set remains English and Bulgarian; unsupported browser locale `de-DE` falls back to English. Android device and real browser integration behavior remain unverified.

## Mixed tree delete confirmation (2026-11-12)

The shared Mixed tree editor now hides Rename and Delete while the control is read-only, and its rename handler rechecks edit permission. Material and Cupertino tree deletion resolve the shared `mixed` Delete confirmation policy, use their native dialog, and guard the target value and current edit permission before committing. A pending dialog is dismissed when the value changes or editing is revoked. Paired mounted tests cover cancellation, read-only revocation during confirmation, and accepting the deletion of a complex child. Full `:examples-compose:jvmTest`, `:examples-compose:iosSimulatorArm64Test`, `:examples-compose:jsBrowserTest` and `:examples-compose:wasmJsBrowserTest` suites each pass 631 tests with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. Mixed type change, combinator branch and composite-cell destructive confirmation remain open.

## Mixed type-change confirmation (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino Mixed controls now route type changes and clearing through the shared `mixed` `typeChange` confirmation decision. A nonempty old object or array prompts by default; `options.confirmation.typeChange: "never"` commits immediately. The selected type and committed data stay unchanged on Cancel. Accept rechecks edit permission, schema type, the original form root and target value, so a host replacement invalidates the pending action even if the child object is reused. Eight paired mounted tests cover confirmation, cancellation, immediate override, changed-child replacement and same-child parent rebind. Full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites each pass 639 tests with no failures. Initial full and focused JS attempts stalled after bundling with less than 165 MiB free; after removing generated iOS binaries and retrying with more than 490 MiB free, focused and full JS suites passed. Browser-only AG Grid, Monaco and TemplateLayout adapters, combinator branch changes and composite-cell destructive confirmation remain open.

## Combinator branch confirmation (2026-11-12)

OneOf and AnyOf now route branch switches through one controller using the shared `branchChange` policy. A present old value prompts by default; `options.confirmation.branchChange: "never"` applies immediately. Cancel leaves data and selected branch unchanged. Confirm rechecks edit permission, the original root, target value and branch index; host replacement dismisses a pending prompt. Material and Cupertino use their respective confirmation dialogs, while Cupertino still delegates the branch selector itself to Material. Both combinator renderers now stack selector and detail in a Column: the previous overlap let a pointer click intended for a tab hit the underlying detail field. Eight paired mounted cases cover OneOf/AnyOf, cancellation, acceptance, host replacement and explicit never. Full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites each pass 647 tests with no failures; Android main compilation passes. AllOf has no branch switch. Composite-cell destructive confirmation and device interaction remain open.

## Composite table-cell deletion (2026-11-12)

Object- and array-valued table cells now expose a guarded Clear action. It resolves `delete` under catalog ID `compositeCell`: the default `complex` policy prompts for a nonempty object or array, immediately clears an empty object, `always` prompts for the empty object, and `never` clears a nonempty value immediately. Confirm rechecks edit permission, the array snapshot, row identity and exact old cell value. A pending prompt is discarded after host replacement or read-only revocation, including when row removal was already disabled by `minItems`. An authored detail draft that clears the root object uses the same cell policy at Apply; canceling keeps committed data and reopens the draft. The Mixed root clear inside that draft separately requests `typeChange` confirmation before the cell-level Apply request.

The first full JVM run had two narrow composite-cell geometry failures: adding Clear beside summary, error marker and Edit crowded a 90 dp column. Clear now stays inline in wider cells and moves below the original summary row below 160 dp. The focused geometry and 16-case delete suites pass. Final full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites each pass 663 tests with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. Real-device focus and accessibility, full confirmation fixture workflows and remaining destructive-operation inventory are open.

## Imported destructive-confirmation fixture (2026-11-12)

The pinned `destructive-confirmation` example is now imported with a native-table adaptation for its AG Grid `charges` control. Its schema description and English/Bulgarian translations describe single-row deletion; data, shared confirmation config and other controls remain upstream. Eight mounted tests load the imported fixture in both Material and Cupertino, covering default confirmation with cancel/accept, a per-control `never` override, adapted table deletion, and Bulgarian dialog text. The full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites each passed 671 tests with no failures. A subsequent description-only adaptation update passed the focused eight JVM cases. Snapshot and coverage checks report 43 imports among 49 classified fixtures. AG Grid batch selection and the remaining fixture operations are not covered by these tests.

## Destructive fixture branch and type coverage (2026-11-12)

Six more mounted fixture cases exercise `contact` oneOf branch confirmation, nonempty `payload` type-change confirmation and simple `scalar` type change without a prompt in Material and Cupertino. The imported `contact` schema exposed a Cupertino registry collision: its generic object tester ranked 102 and intercepted an object-typed oneOf (and anyOf) before the combinator tester ranked 3, which led to recursive rendering and a Compose node lifecycle failure. Cupertino oneOf and anyOf now receive the same +100 family rank offset as its object and allOf testers. A registry test checks both typed-object combinators select the correct renderer; the direct-delegation inventory now reflects their native confirmation-dialog wrappers. Full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites pass 677 tests each; Cupertino JVM tests and Android main compilation pass. The renderer-registry check passes. Remaining fixture operations include dynamic-property, expandable/ListWithDetail, mixed-tree and composite-cell deletes.

## Destructive fixture delete operations (2026-11-12)

Twelve additional Material/Cupertino mounted fixture cases now exercise dynamic-property deletion, expandable-array item deletion, ListWithDetail item deletion, nested Mixed-tree deletion, nonempty composite-cell deletion, and empty composite-cell deletion under the configured `complex` policy. The confirmation cases cancel without a change and accept exactly once; the empty composite object clears without a prompt. The mixed-tree test verifies the `assignment` subtree is removed while `supplier` remains. Full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites each pass 689 tests; Android main compilation passes. The coverage checker still reports 49 classified fixtures, 43 imported, six explicit gaps/exclusions.

The imported `contact` oneOf control does not yet expose a selector-level Clear action. The visible field-level Clear empties `email` and correctly does not invoke branch-change confirmation. §18.28 requires a separate action that leaves no branch selected and preserves enclosing properties, so oneOf clearing remains open even though branch switching is tested. AG Grid batch deletion remains outside this native-table adaptation.

## OneOf selector Clear and enclosing-field preservation (2026-11-12)

Generic oneOf controls now expose a selector-level Clear action using the shared `branchChange` confirmation policy. Cancel preserves data and selection; acceptance removes branch-specific data and the branch form without automatically selecting another branch. Enclosing properties survive both Clear and branch switches, taking precedence over generated branch defaults. Selecting a branch after Clear initializes it without asking to discard the preserved enclosing fields. A selected branch with no branch-specific data can still be cleared. `options.clearable: false`, read-only revocation and host replacement guard the action. The Clear action uses the shared localized accessible name, and Material/Cupertino use their family confirmation dialogs. The selector action is currently visible whenever it is available; the §18.28 focus/hover-only visual affordance and device keyboard/focus behavior remain to verify.

The imported destructive-confirmation fixture now has 28 mounted tests, including selector Clear in both families. Twelve additional focused oneOf tests cover preservation, no reselection, empty selected branch, opt-out, host replacement and read-only revocation. Full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites each pass 703 tests, and Android main compilation passes.

## Generic anyOf tabs preserve data (2026-11-12)

The pinned spec defines generic anyOf tabs as views over one bound value. The renderer previously routed tab clicks through the branch-change controller, replacing the value with generated defaults and requesting confirmation. Tab clicks now change only the displayed editor, including while read-only. Editing the selected tab still writes to the same scoped value. The corresponding Material/Cupertino mounted tests cover navigation without data changes, editing a second branch while retaining the first branch's data, read-only navigation, and navigation when the scoped value is absent. OneOf still uses destructive branch-change confirmation. The Cupertino anyOf entry is again a direct Material-view delegate; the renderer inventory check passes. Full examples-compose JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites each pass 705 tests, and Android main compilation passes. The prior combinator confirmation log entry described anyOf as a destructive branch switch and is superseded for generic tabs by this checkpoint.

## OneOf Clear focus and hover affordance (2026-11-12)

The generic oneOf selector now fades its Clear icon until the selector or Clear button has keyboard focus, the pointer hovers either surface, or touch mode is active. The action stays mounted and accessible; its confirmation, no-selection state and mutation guards are unchanged. Focus and hover state is read by the graphics layer rather than composition: composing a button on selector focus canceled the ExposedDropdownMenuBox click before its menu opened. A JVM image test confirms hidden, selector hover, pointer movement onto Clear, pointer exit and keyboard focus states. Two new paired Material/Cupertino mounted tests activate Clear with Enter and verify its accessible name and confirmation. Real-device visual and pointer behavior remains to verify.

## Shared input and choice clearable option (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino text-input components now honor local `options.clearable: false`, which also covers their numeric and temporal inputs. Material enum and oneOf enum dropdowns and Cupertino's shared choice selector now apply the same option and hide Clear while read-only or disabled. Choice Clear tests use the actual scoped data's presence rather than a matched option, so an imported invalid choice can be repaired. Ten new mounted tests (five Material/Cupertino pairs) cover text, integer, enum and oneOf enum opt-out, read-only revocation, required-text clearing, and clearing unmatched enum/oneOf values. The required text clear removes its property, leaving schema validation to report the missing requirement. Full examples-compose suites pass 744 JVM tests (including the JVM-only oneOf image test) and 743 tests each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser; Android main compilation passes. ## Choice Clear focus and hover affordance (2026-11-12)

Material enum and oneOf enum dropdowns and Cupertino's shared choice selector now fade Clear until the selector or button has focus, either surface is hovered, or touch mode is active. The button stays mounted, so focus/hover updates are read by its graphics layer and do not disrupt the menu anchor. Four JVM image tests verify hidden, selector hover, button hover, pointer exit and focus states for both variants and families. Six new shared mounted cases verify Material autocomplete/Cupertino menu selection, Space activation of the focused Clear button, and clearability of present `0` and empty-string enum values. Full examples-compose suites pass 754 JVM tests (including five JVM image tests total) and 749 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser; Android main compilation passes. Boolean checkbox/toggle controls follow a separate renderer path, and their clear behavior and real-device pointer visuals remain unverified.

## Boolean presence and Clear contract (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino checkbox and switch controls now resolve the actual stored boolean without coercing the string `"false"`. Missing, null and invalid values show a localized “Not set” indication; Material checkboxes also use the indeterminate state. Rendering these values emits no change. A present `false` exposes Clear by default; `options.clearable: false`, read-only and disabled states hide it. Clearing an object property removes it, while clearing an array position writes `false` without shifting the array. The action uses the shared localized accessible name and focus/hover/touch visual affordance. English and Bulgarian are the only shipped locale defaults; unsupported `de-DE` falls back to English.

Two shared state tests cover object and array presence, invalid strings and the array write result. Twelve paired mounted UI cases cover false, missing, invalid, opt-out, read-only and a live English-to-Bulgarian switch in checkbox/switch families. Full suites pass core JVM 404 and iOS simulator 403, Material JVM 51, Cupertino JVM 5, and examples-compose JVM 766, iOS simulator 761, JS browser 761 and Wasm browser 761, all with zero failures. Affected iOS/JS/Wasm and Android main compilation passes; the renderer registry, spec coverage (46 of 49 imported), and whitespace checks pass. Physical-device pointer/focus visuals and array-position mounted UI remain to verify.

## Boolean array position and visual affordance (2026-11-12)

An authored two-position boolean tuple now has mounted Material/Cupertino coverage for Clear on the first position: `[true,true]` becomes `[false,true]` in one change. Four JVM image tests check checkbox and switch Clear visibility in both families while hidden, hovered over the widget, hovered over the button, after pointer exit and after keyboard focus. Full examples-compose suites pass 772 JVM tests and 763 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, all with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. Physical-device pointer/focus visuals and the boolean table-cell error/correction contract remain to verify.

The Material indeterminate checkbox now exposes the localized “Not set” accessibility state as well as its visual state. Two mounted Material/Cupertino tests render an invalid string `"false"` in the first Active column of an ordinary two-row table, find that cell's error indicator, correct the widget to a real boolean in one data change, and confirm the indicator and “Not set” state disappear without changing the second row. Full suites pass core JVM 404, Material JVM 51, Cupertino JVM 5, and examples-compose JVM 774, iOS simulator 765, JS browser 765 and Wasm browser 765, all with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. ## Localized boolean cell feedback and dynamic Clear (2026-11-12)

The shared table error indicator now exposes its validation messages through accessibility state using the same text as its tooltip. Ordinary table cell indicators pass their raw errors through the configured error translator with the cell UI schema before display. The paired boolean table tests use a Bulgarian error translation and confirm the first row's accessible indicator contains the localized message, then disappears after correcting the invalid string value. Two further Material/Cupertino mounted cases confirm a dynamic additional-property boolean Clear retains its key with `false`; only the explicit Delete action removes the key. Full suites pass core JVM 404, Material JVM 51, Cupertino JVM 5, and examples-compose JVM 776, iOS simulator 767, JS browser 767 and Wasm browser 767, all with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. ## Required false and boolean cell tooltip (2026-11-12)

Four Material/Cupertino mounted cases cover checkbox and switch with a required boolean property: stored `false` is valid, Clear removes the property and makes the form invalid, and interacting with the unanswered control stores `true` and restores validity. Two JVM pointer tests hover the invalid boolean table cell's indicator and confirm a second copy of its Bulgarian validation message appears in the tooltip; the first copy is the inline error text. Full suites pass core JVM 404, Material JVM 51, Cupertino JVM 5, and examples-compose JVM 782, iOS simulator 771, JS browser 771 and Wasm browser 771, all with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. The registry, spec coverage (46 of 49 imported), and whitespace checks pass. Physical-device pointer/focus behavior and a true-only `const` field's mounted interaction remain to verify.

## Boolean agreement and numeric draft storage (2026-11-12)

Four mounted Material/Cupertino checkbox/switch cases verify that an explicit `const: true` makes stored `false` invalid, clicking the widget stores `true` and restores validity, and clicking again stores `false` and restores the constraint error. Numeric integer and number controls in both families now keep incomplete, nonfinite, out-of-range and decimal-precision-losing text in local drafts. They mark combined validity Pending and publish no string or invented numeric value; correction publishes a numeric value once, and a host replacement discards the stale draft. The shared parser accepts integers only within ±9007199254740991 and compares decimal text with the converted finite Double's decimal value before committing a number. The supported limits are documented in README.

Two pure numeric parser tests pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. Four mounted numeric scenarios pass on each target, including safe-range and precision-loss rejection. Full suites pass core JVM 406 and iOS/JS/Wasm 405 each, Material JVM 51, Cupertino JVM 5, extended JVM 315, and examples-compose JVM 790 and iOS/JS/Wasm 779 each, all with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. Registry, spec coverage (46 of 49 imported) and whitespace checks pass. Numeric step/multipleOf and bounds behavior, slider, formatted-number policy, numeric type intent, pending-owner disposal and physical-device interaction remain open.

## Numeric read-only draft revocation (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino integer/number controls now restore the last committed value and release their Pending owner when the control becomes read-only or disabled. Four mounted cases begin an invalid draft, switch the form to read-only, and verify Valid status, the original numeric data, no change callback, committed text and no local error. Full suites pass core JVM 406, Material JVM 51, Cupertino JVM 5, and examples-compose JVM 794, iOS simulator 783, JS browser 783 and Wasm browser 783, all with zero failures; Android main compilation passes. Registry, spec coverage (46 of 49 imported) and whitespace checks pass. Disabled-rule transitions and pending-owner disposal on editor removal still need direct tests, along with the broader numeric step/bounds/slider contracts.

## Numeric disabled rule and editor removal (2026-11-12)

Eight more mounted cases cover Material/Cupertino integer and number controls. Four start an invalid local draft, change a separate `lock` property so a `DISABLE` rule applies, then verify the committed text is restored, Pending clears, and the numeric data is unchanged without a change callback. Four mount the actual numeric editor under a stable `JsonFormsStateProvider`, remove only the editor, and verify its pending owner is disposed while the form state and data remain intact. Full examples-compose suites pass 802 JVM and 791 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with zero failures or skips. Numeric step, bounds, slider, formatting and numeric type intent remain open.

## Numeric range restriction and repair (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino integer/number controls now consult shared `restrict` precedence for completed typed edits against inclusive and exclusive schema bounds. A new out-of-range edit remains local with Pending validity and a localized English/Bulgarian range error; an already out-of-range value remains visible and edits moving toward the range can commit without clamping. `restrict: false` permits the numeric edit while schema validation still reports it. One core matrix covers inclusive/exclusive, legacy boolean exclusivity, opt-out, rejection and repair. Eight mounted cases cover both renderer families and types, unrestricted schema validation and Bulgarian feedback. Full core suites pass 407 JVM and 406 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser (13 existing skips per target); Material 51 and Cupertino five JVM tests pass. Full examples-compose suites pass 810 JVM and 799 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with zero failures or skips; Android main compiles. Numeric step/multipleOf, slider, formatted-number policy, numeric type intent and physical-device interaction remain open.

## Numeric step actions (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino integer/number inputs now expose localized increment and decrement actions. Shared step policy resolves explicit `options.step` before suitable schema `multipleOf`, then integer `1` or number `0.1`; a nonpositive, nonfinite or fractional integer explicit step reports a configuration error and disables the actions. Decimal stepping uses scaled integers and declines results outside the portable exact representation rather than exposing binary floating artifacts. Bound guards also disable a disallowed step; step choices do not redefine schema `multipleOf` validation. One core step matrix and six mounted action cases cover resolution, decimal math, portable integer ceiling, multipleOf, explicit override, schema validity, bounds, and invalid integer configuration. A locale test confirms English/Bulgarian action labels and English fallback for unsupported German. Full core suites pass 409 JVM and 408 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser (13 existing skips per target); Material 51 and Cupertino five JVM tests pass. Full examples-compose suites pass 816 JVM and 805 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with zero failures or skips; Android main compiles. Slider adaptation, formatted-number policy, numeric type intent and physical-device interaction remain open.

## Zero-based slider positions (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino sliders now share an exact-decimal position profile derived from zero-based schema `multipleOf`, inclusive/exclusive bounds and the numeric type. The native widgets move over position indexes, so the spec's integer example selects 2, 4 and 6 rather than 1, 3 and 5; integer schemas with fractional multiples retain integer data. Both sliders expose a current-value indication and accessibility state description, show “Not set” for missing data with only a provisional default thumb, preserve and display invalid incoming data separately, and Clear ordinary properties without recommitting the default. One-position ranges use an explicit Select value action; no-position, invalid-range, invalid-step, unsupported-precision and more-than-10,001-position cases report diagnostics rather than manufacturing data. Five core profile cases, ten mounted Material/Cupertino cases and a locale test cover this behavior. Full core suites pass 415 JVM and 414 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser (13 existing skips per target); Material 51 and Cupertino five JVM tests pass. Full examples-compose suites pass 826 JVM and 815 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with zero failures or skips; Android main compiles. Narrow viewport and physical-device slider gestures, formatted-number policy and numeric type intent remain open.

The final single-position Cupertino presentation uses the native Cupertino button. Its mounted JVM scenario passes, and the renderer compiles on JVM, iOS simulator, JS and Wasm.

## Numeric mixed-editor intent (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino number/integer union controls now treat an editor switch as presentation state when the stored value is numeric: neither renderer replaces the value or invokes destructive confirmation, and an explicitly selected numeric editor survives subsequent numeric edits. Selecting integer over a fractional value exposes the local integer error while the original union document remains Valid; correction stores an integer without silent rounding. One core mapper case and paired mounted cases cover preservation, fractional repair, reverse switching and later decimal editing. Full core suites pass 416 JVM and 415 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser (13 existing skips per target); Material 51 and Cupertino five JVM tests pass. Full examples-compose suites pass 828 JVM and 817 each on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with zero failures or skips; Android main compiles. A final focused mounted JVM rerun also passes with authored `typeChange: "always"`, proving that numeric editor switching bypasses the prompt even under that policy. The spec defines no portable serialized formatter/parser callback: JSON `options.format` remains a string option, while a browser-host runtime callback registry would require separate capability and interaction tests.

## Temporal range controls (2026-11-12)

Five pure bound tests and 16 mounted temporal tests cover literal `formatMinimum`, `formatExclusiveMinimum`, `formatMaximum` and `formatExclusiveMaximum` at date, time and date-time precision. Material and Cupertino typed out-of-range values commit with range validation errors; restricted pickers disable confirmation for invalid dates and whole-minute candidates, while `restrict: false` permits confirmation. The date-time date step accepts a date only when it contains a selectable whole minute. English and Bulgarian messages are covered. The five pure JVM tests and all 16 mounted tests pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips. The full iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser suites and Android main compilation passed during this change. Dynamic `$data` bounds and offset-specific picker interaction remain open.

## Temporal picker feedback follow-up (2026-11-12)

Both renderer families now show a translated range explanation within an open date, time or date-time picker when a restricted selection disables confirmation. The explanation is local to the draft, disappears on Cancel, stays absent with `restrict: false`, and updates when the locale or a host schema bound changes. Open picker state no longer resets merely because the schema changes; typed bound messages use the active display format. Six pure Material bound tests pass. The full affected JVM suites pass 57 Material, five Cupertino and 854 examples-compose tests with no failures or skips. All 26 mounted temporal tests pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with no failures or skips; Android main compiles. Dynamic `$data` bounds, explicit accessibility association in picker semantics, device interaction and offset edge cases remain open.

## Temporal `$data` bound follow-up (2026-11-12)

The six Material/Cupertino temporal controls now resolve string-valued `$data` bounds against current form data using Ajv-style absolute and relative JSON Pointers. Pointer cases cover array paths, escaped and empty property names, and relative property-name tokens. Live mounted cases show typed data becoming Invalid against a sibling bound, then becoming Valid while an open picker updates when the host relaxes that sibling. A controller-state read fixes a stale props snapshot observed on the iOS simulator. The 10 pure Material bound tests and 32 mounted temporal tests pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with no failures or skips. Full affected JVM suites pass 61 Material, five Cupertino and 860 examples-compose tests; Android main compiles. The combined JS gate exhausted disk while generating KLIB caches; the isolated rerun passed after generated-cache cleanup. Non-string referenced bounds do not yet reproduce Ajv's invalid-bound validation error, and validator-level `$data` outside these controls remains open.

## Temporal invalid `$data` bound follow-up (2026-11-12)

The temporal resolver now distinguishes an absent `$data` target from a present target of the wrong type. Number, null, array and object targets produce a keyword-specific renderer-owned validation error; restricted picker confirmation stays disabled. Host replacement with a string bound clears the error and re-enables a valid choice. Paired mounted Material/Cupertino tests cover English and Bulgarian error text, error counts and recovery. The 11 pure Material bound tests and 34 mounted temporal tests pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with no failures or skips. Full affected JVM suites pass 62 Material, five Cupertino and 862 examples-compose tests; Android main compiles. General validator-level `$data` support and malformed reference diagnostics remain open. Generated browser KLIB caches were cleared after the checks to recover disk space.

## Temporal picker accessibility semantics (2026-11-12)

Both renderer families now attach the localized range explanation as Compose error semantics to the disabled picker confirmation action. The error is absent with `restrict: false` and clears when a live schema bound permits the current selection. Existing mounted tests now assert this state on date, time and date-time dialogs. All 34 focused temporal tests pass on JVM and iOS simulator. The focused JS browser rerun exhausted disk during Kotlin JS cache generation after the bundle compiled; its generated cache was removed. JS/Wasm runtime, physical screen-reader behavior and editor-to-error association remain unverified for this change. OptimumCode's external assertion context supplies the current instance path, but no root document, so validator-level `$data` for hidden/unmounted controls needs a broader design than a simple custom keyword.

## Temporal editor accessibility semantics (2026-11-12)

The shared Material and Cupertino text inputs now expose their visible field error on the editable semantics node. The six paired typed date, time and date-time mounted tests assert the range message and its removal after a valid replacement. The full 34-case temporal class passes on JVM and iOS simulator with zero failures or skips; Android main compiles. JS/Wasm runtime and direct screen-reader behavior remain unverified for this edit. The shipped locale bundles remain English and Bulgarian; existing core tests assert that unsupported `de-DE` falls back to English.

## Validator format assertions and Time storage (2026-11-12)

The built-in OptimumCode loader now requests format assertions, including for current-draft schemas. Nested invalid email and URI values, and a Time value missing its offset, produce core `format` errors even without a mounted control. A valid email, URI and `09:30:00Z` pass. Both temporal renderer families now use the spec's `HH:mm:ssZ` Time save default and continue to parse prior offset-free values for editing. The full JVM suites pass 418 core, 62 Material, five Cupertino and 862 examples-compose cases. The 13 focused core validator cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser; 34 focused temporal mounted cases pass on iOS simulator; Android main and examples-compose JS/Wasm main compile. Mounted JS/Wasm interaction, general `$data` bounds, custom format extensions and actual timezone conversion for Time remain open. [OptimumCode format behavior](https://github.com/OptimumCode/json-schema-validator#format-assertion) defaults newer drafts to annotation; [Ajv formats](https://github.com/ajv-validator/ajv-formats/blob/master/README.md#formats) requires an offset for `time`.

## Validator-level temporal format bounds (2026-11-12)

The four temporal comparison keywords now use OptimumCode external assertions for date, time and date-time schema formats. Literal and absolute/relative `$data` bounds validate nested or unrendered values using exact instance/schema paths. Tests cover array siblings, escaped pointers, relative property-name tokens, internal `$ref`, conditional branches, present wrong-type versus missing references, root replacement, inclusive/exclusive bounds, timezone offsets and fractional time precision. The renderer still shows localized draft feedback while schema errors carry document validity. Full affected JVM suites pass 423 core, 62 Material, five Cupertino and 864 examples-compose tests. The 18 focused validator cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser; 36 focused mounted temporal cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Android, JS and Wasm main compile. The first iOS mounted link exhausted disk; the identical rerun passed after clearing generated build outputs. Dynamic-bound schemas recompile per validation to capture an immutable root because the upstream experimental assertion context exposes only the current instance path. General `$data` keywords, malformed bound/reference diagnostics, other formats, browser-mounted behavior and physical device interaction remain open. [OptimumCode custom assertions](https://github.com/OptimumCode/json-schema-validator#custom-assertions) provide the portable extension point.

## Button width and §14 inventory (2026-11-12)

The shared extended Button renderer now applies boolean `options.fullWidth` to Material and Cupertino buttons. A mounted test for each family checks intrinsic sizing when omitted or false, filled cell width when true, the secondary style, an unchanged parent cell width, and rejection of `block` as an alias. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 317 tests with no failures or skips. The changed renderer compiles for iOS simulator, JS and Wasm, and examples-compose Android main compiles. The pinned §14 Button-width paragraph is classified as normative with partial JVM evidence, leaving 99 keyword candidates for manual review. Nested grid/flex allocation, mounted non-JVM behavior and device interaction remain open.

## Button action dispatch and pending state (2026-11-12)

The shared Material/Cupertino Button renderer now selects explicit top-level `action`, then a stable `name` when neither action nor script is present, then the empty command identifier for an unnamed button. A script-only button remains disabled with an English/Bulgarian unsupported diagnostic; when action and script coexist, action dispatches and a localized authoring warning is reported. The host handler is suspendable, and a synchronous guard plus pending state prevents duplicate activation until it finishes. Four mounted JVM cases cover command precedence, params, ignored option aliases, unsupported script, pending completion and repeat activation. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 321 tests and the full examples-compose JVM suite passes 870 tests, with no failures or skips. Webcomponent JS, iOS simulator, Wasm and Android main compile. The action-context interface, webcomponent listener promise completion, rejection propagation tests, and native/browser interaction remain open; the ledger records only partial evidence. The candidate inventory now has 97 paragraphs awaiting classification.

## Web component action completion (2026-11-12)

The web component's bubbling `jsonforms-action` event now exposes `detail.waitUntil(promise)`. Listeners register asynchronous host work during dispatch; the suspendable Kotlin action handler waits for all registered promises, so the shared Button pending guard covers host completion. A rejected registered promise propagates through the handler and its `finally` clears pending. Four Node tests pass for registration, refresh, event details, pending completion, late registration refusal and promise rejection. The webcomponent Kotlin/JS compilation passes. Mounted browser behavior, the richer spec action context and native rejection interaction remain unverified.

## Mounted JS Button actions and width (2026-11-12)

All six `ButtonActionUiTest` and `ButtonWidthUiTest` methods pass in the JS browser with no skips or failures, covering both Material and Cupertino. An initial class-name filter exited successfully but selected no methods; the explicit method-name rerun is the evidence. The matching Wasm browser attempt stopped during test compilation with `No space left on device`, so no Wasm Button test executed. Generated extended-renderers JS and Wasm KLIB caches were removed after the failure, restoring workspace space. The webcomponent itself still needs a mounted browser test of `waitUntil` completion and rejection, and iOS/Wasm interaction remains open.

## Semantic Button colors (2026-11-12)

The shared Button renderer now maps all six portable names through Material and Cupertino native styles: primary/success use filled accent, secondary uses an outline or native tint, alternative uses a tonal or gray treatment, warning uses an outlined/tinted warning accent, and error uses the theme's destructive container. Invalid literal color strings fall back to primary. A common test covers each name and fallback; two mounted JVM screenshot tests verify that primary and error containers draw from custom blue/red theme tokens in each family. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 324 tests with zero failures or skips. JS, Wasm, iOS simulator and Android main compile. Warning, alternative, secondary and success visual contrast across light/dark themes, plus native/browser mounted color output, remain open.

## Decorative Button icons (2026-11-12)

Both Button families now render a top-level icon string as visible text before the label, with an 8 dp gap and decorative accessibility semantics. An `options.icon` value is ignored. Paired mounted tests verify the icon increases intrinsic width, stays out of the accessible button name, preserves action dispatch, and disappears when removed. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 326 tests; the two focused JS browser methods execute with zero failures or skips. JS, Wasm, iOS simulator and Android main compile. The generated JS KLIB cache was removed after the browser check to recover disk space. Icon-only label fallback, visual glyph rendering on native devices, and Wasm browser interaction remain open.

## Button disabled and strict boolean options (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino Buttons now honor boolean `options.disabled` in addition to inherited form read-only. The activation callback rechecks that eligibility before dispatch. A mounted test in each family covers live true/false changes, a read-only transition, and an action that resumes when editing is restored. The tests exposed that `JsonPrimitive.booleanOrNull` accepts the string `"true"`; Button `disabled`, `readonly` and `fullWidth` now require JSON booleans, and the width tests cover the string case. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 328 tests. Four focused JS browser methods execute with zero failures or skips. JS, Wasm, iOS simulator and Android main compile; the generated JS KLIB cache was removed afterward. The §14 command-versus-navigation paragraph is now classified, leaving 96 keyword candidates for review. Keyboard focus/activation, nested layout geometry and native/Wasm interactions remain open.

## URL and presentation requirement audit (2026-11-12)

The pinned §12 URL-template paragraph and §13 ImageView/Link paragraphs are classified, leaving 93 keyword candidates for review. URL-template substitution remains not verified because the `$dynamic.template` overlay profile is not implemented; its percent encoding and final policy check must land with that profile. Existing ImageView resolution already covers defined direct/empty/refused source precedence, array-item scope, string-only values and URL policy. Focused JVM reruns pass three core source cases, 14 mounted ImageView cases and five mounted Link cases, all without failures or skips. The ImageView ledger status is corrected from not-verified to partial; dynamic overlays and target image/navigation interaction remain open.

An attempted native Link role override was reverted: Compose UI 1.12.2 has no `Role.Link` constant, so the Material/Cupertino link presentation still exposes its native button role while performing navigation. The five focused Link JVM tests pass again after the revert. Accessibility role parity requires a separate platform approach rather than an unsupported common-role value.

The §13.5 language-resolution clause is now classified with partial fallback evidence. Seven focused TemplateLayout JVM tests pass: authored payload preservation, explicit/configured/default language selection, and distinct unknown-language, disabled-evaluation and unavailable-engine diagnostics across both renderer families. This does not implement a template engine; the DOM host and cleanup proof in the web-only review remain required. The ledger now has 282 rows and 92 keyword candidates await review.

## Button pending presentation (2026-11-12)

Material Buttons now show a native circular progress indicator and Cupertino Buttons show the installed native activity indicator while their awaited action handler runs. The indicator is decorative; the disabled Button exposes a localized `button.running` state description in English or Bulgarian, which updates while pending. The two existing suspended-handler UI tests now assert both languages and successful re-enablement. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 328 tests, and JS, Wasm, iOS simulator and Android main compile. Browser/native mounted indicator behavior and rejected-handler UI remain open.

A focused JS browser rerun of the two pending-action methods was interrupted after nearly two minutes without a method result. Its generated Gradle report contains one skipped placeholder, zero executed methods and no failures; it is not browser interaction evidence. The generated extended-renderers JS KLIB cache was removed afterward to recover disk space. The pending indicator's infinite animation is a possible test-clock cause, but the run did not establish the cause.

A non-pending `buttonSemanticColorsKeepAllPortableNames` JS browser method then executed and passed, confirming the runner itself starts. The suspended-action tests now pause and manually advance the Compose test clock; their focused JVM run passes. A subsequent focused JS rerun stopped during Kotlin's generated module-cache write with `No space left on device`, before browser execution. Its incomplete generated JS cache was removed. JS pending-indicator interaction remains unverified; the interrupted run and disk failure do not count as test passes.

The full extended-renderers JVM rerun after the test-clock change passes 328 tests with zero failures or skips. `scripts/check-spec-candidates.mjs` reports 180 candidate paragraphs, with 92 awaiting classification; `git diff --check` passes. A source scan confirms that the shipped locale defaults remain English and Bulgarian. Tests using `de-DE` assert English fallback; they do not add a German bundle.

## Button keyboard activation (2026-11-12)

Paired Material and Cupertino mounted tests now focus the Button and verify its native Button role, Enter and Space activation, and ignored key input after `options.disabled` becomes true. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 330 tests with zero failures or skips. Both focused JS browser keyboard tests pass in Chrome Headless. iOS, Wasm and Android keyboard interaction remains unverified.

After reclaiming generated output from the webcomponent and examples-compose modules, the two pending-action JS browser cases pass with the controlled test clock. The complete extended-renderers JS browser suite passes 322 tests with zero failures or skips, including both keyboard and pending cases. The generated JS KLIB cache was removed afterward to restore disk space; test reports remain. This supersedes the earlier interrupted and disk-full JS attempts above. The test asserts the pending accessibility description and disabled state; visual indicator pixels and webcomponent promise completion in a mounted page remain unverified.

## Button action-event context (2026-11-12)

The shared Button now prefers an event-based host callback carrying action, translated label, normalized params, triggering element/path and a form-context snapshot (data, schema, root UI schema, validation errors, read-only state, locale and config). The older two-argument handler remains available. Paired Material/Cupertino mounted tests verify callback precedence, payload fidelity and fresh data/locale after host updates. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 332 tests with zero failures or skips. The webcomponent uses the event callback and exposes the serializable context fields on `jsonforms-action`; five Node dispatcher tests pass, and `:webcomponent:compileKotlinJs` passes.

A focused JS browser run for the new event tests produced its Kotlin test IR but no browser result after several minutes and was interrupted. Its generated JS KLIB cache was removed to recover disk space. The new event callback's JS runtime behavior, mounted webcomponent dispatch, and Wasm/iOS interaction remain unverified; the earlier 322-test JS result predates this change.

The stalled JS run was followed by clearing 884 MB of regenerable Gradle expanded ZIP cache. The focused action-event tests then passed in Chrome Headless. Complete extended-renderer suites pass 332 JVM, 324 JS browser, 324 iOS simulator and 324 Wasm browser tests, all with zero failures or skips. Both Wasm and iOS simulator main compilation pass. This supersedes the earlier new-event target-runtime gap; mounted webcomponent dispatch, Android interaction, script evaluation and indicator pixel appearance remain open.

## Web component action settlement (2026-11-12)

The `jsonforms-action` dispatcher now waits for every promise registered with `detail.waitUntil`, even if one rejects early, and then propagates the first rejection. A new Node test holds a second host task open after the first rejects and proves dispatch remains pending until the second settles. All six registration/dispatch tests pass. The development webpack bundle compiles with four existing warnings after generated Wasm/iOS/Material caches were cleared to provide space; an initial bundle attempt failed with `ENOSPC`. Mounted button behavior inside the custom element remains unverified.

## Mounted web component action lifecycle (2026-11-12)

A new Node/Chrome DevTools integration test serves the actual development bundle, waits for the rendered Button accessibility node, clicks it, and checks the bubbling event's action, params, label, element and context. It holds `waitUntil` work open to prove duplicate clicks do not dispatch, then resolves the work and confirms the Button can dispatch again. The rejection extension initially exposed a real failure: the coroutine scope became unusable after a host promise rejected. Button dispatch now runs in a composition-owned supervisor scope, so the rejection reaches browser error handling without canceling later actions. The mounted test then proves a rejected promise remains pending while another registered task runs, reports the rejection, and allows a later action. The one mounted Chrome test and six dispatcher Node tests pass. The full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 332 tests; Wasm and iOS simulator main compilations and the webcomponent development webpack build pass (four webpack warnings). Visual indicator pixels, Android interaction and script evaluation remain open.

The webcomponent action bridge now finds the triggering Button in the parsed UI-schema tree and returns its exact authored JSON object in `detail.element`. The mounted Chrome test updates the demo schema with a visibility rule and an extension field, then confirms both survive the action event alongside params and context. Programmatically constructed Buttons retain the existing reconstructed-element fallback. The development webpack build and mounted test pass after this change.

## Opt-in Button script execution (2026-11-12)

The shared Button now accepts a host `ExtendedScriptHandler` only when `jsonformsExtended.security.allowScriptEvaluation` is explicitly true. Without a host executor it retains the unsupported fallback; with an executor but without permission it is disabled and reports localized `button.evaluationDisabled`. String `"true"` and numeric `1` are denied in paired JVM tests, and the mounted Chrome test also denies the string value. An action still wins over a coauthored script. The script path shares the pending guard and composition-owned supervisor scope. The webcomponent accepts a JSON `config` attribute, runs a string script as an async function body with the action event bound as `this`, and reports compile/CSP failures through `jsonforms-diagnostic` without changing CSP. Its `$el` points to the custom-element host. Paired Material/Cupertino mounted JVM tests cover gate changes, params, pending completion and action precedence; the full extended-renderers JVM suite passes 334 tests. Both focused iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser tests pass with two cases each. Eight Node registration/executor tests pass, and a mounted Chrome test covers the default-denied gate, top-level await, event bindings, duplicate suppression, script rejection reporting and subsequent recovery. The webcomponent development webpack build passes with four warnings. The §14.2 script-policy paragraph is classified as normative, leaving 91 keyword candidates. A later mounted Chrome rerun adds a restrictive `script-src 'self'` CSP meta policy after loading the development bundle, proves script compilation is blocked with `button.evaluationDisabled`, and confirms the Button recovers for another attempt without running the script. CSP response-header deployment and Android interaction remain unverified.

## Button width inside allocated layout cells (2026-11-12)

Paired mounted Material and Cupertino tests place a Button in a one-column grid cell next to a Button spanning three columns, then repeat with one-weight and three-weight flex cells. Enabling `fullWidth` fills the first cell while both cell bounds and the neighboring Button bounds remain unchanged. All six `ButtonWidthUiTest` methods pass on JVM and iOS simulator, with zero failures or skips. A focused Wasm browser run reached the test task but produced no method result after several minutes and was interrupted. Its generated Wasm KLIB cache and compile-sync output were removed, restoring about 375 MiB of disk space. Wasm interaction remains unverified.

## TemplateLayout normative boundary audit (2026-11-12)

Four §13.5 keyword paragraphs are classified into ten requirement rows covering live bindings, schema-error versus combined-validity messaging, collision-safe child names, original child identity/path, template-local state, script permission, engine capability documentation and guarded two-way writes. The current webcomponent has no disposable DOM template host, so engine-dependent rows remain explicitly unsupported. The fallback's trust-gate test now proves that enabling `$dynamic` alone or passing string `"true"` does not enable execution. All seven focused `UnsupportedTemplateLayoutTest` JVM methods pass. The spec candidate check reports 87 paragraphs awaiting classification; example coverage remains 49 classified, 46 imported and three explicit gaps/exclusions.

## Read-only rule precedence and text inspection (2026-11-12)

The core resolver now applies only `ENABLE`/`DISABLE` rules to enabled state and only `READONLY`/`WRITABLE` rules to read-only state. An unrelated `SHOW` rule no longer erases a read-only option or schema annotation; matched and unmatched read-only rules remain two-way decisions, while form-wide read-only wins. Material and Cupertino shared text inputs now pass their resolved enabled state separately from the read-only flag, allowing inspection when `separateReadonlyFromDisabled` is true while keeping Clear unavailable. Paired mounted tests verify live `WRITABLE` changes, a real clear dispatch, and disabled versus enabled read-only text. The full JVM suites pass 431 core tests (13 pre-existing skips) and 874 Compose example tests (zero skips), with no failures or errors. Focused iOS simulator runs pass 10 core methods and four mounted UI methods. The §15.1 clause is classified, leaving 86 candidate paragraphs. Family-wide separation remains explicitly unsupported pending other-control and target interaction evidence.

## Moved async error owner target (2026-11-12)

The Compose controller now checks both request version and its current target path before accepting an async additional-error result. After an owner moves, a result for its old path is discarded while pending validity remains visible; a result for the current path is accepted. The default unspecified target remains compatible with existing callers. Twelve focused controller methods pass on both JVM and iOS simulator with zero failures or skips. Three §15.5 paragraphs are classified into four requirement rows, leaving 83 normative candidates awaiting review. Row-renderer identity and JS/Wasm/Android interaction remain open.

## Combined-validity host notifications (2026-11-12)

`CombinedValidity` now carries pending, stale and validation mode alongside its status and error counts. Pending changes therefore produce a new snapshot even when an existing error keeps the status Invalid; changing Show to Hide also produces a new snapshot without a data edit. The Compose provider keys `onValidityChange` to current schema and additional error lists, so replacing one error with another at the same count still notifies the host. Mounted callback tests verify these transitions, `NoValidation` with and without a host error, and zero data-change callbacks. The full JVM suites pass 432 core tests (13 existing skips) and 876 Compose example tests (zero skips), with no failures or errors. Focused iOS simulator runs pass five core validity tests and two mounted callback tests. Three §15.3–15.4 keyword paragraphs are classified into five ledger rows; 80 candidates await review. JS/Wasm/Android callbacks and the independent `restrict` clause remain open.

## Restrict independent of validation mode (2026-11-12)

Paired Material and Cupertino mounted tests now show that `NoValidation` still prevents text input beyond `maxLength` and array selection beyond `minItems`/`maxItems`. Local `restrict: false` permits an invalid value while validity remains Unvalidated; switching back to `ValidateAndShow` reports Invalid without another data edit. The focused JVM string and enum-array classes pass nine and eight methods, and all four new methods pass on iOS simulator, with no failures or skips. A repeated identical text replacement after a rejected edit did not issue another Compose change event, so the test uses a distinct second value to exercise the live restriction switch. Action permissions, other controls and JS/Wasm/Android interaction remain open.

Paired mounted Button tests confirm that `NoValidation` does not bypass form read-only or `options.disabled`, while switching validation mode preserves action dispatch and causes no data change. Both new Button methods pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Other control types and JS/Wasm/Android interaction remain open.

## Conditional rules and schema validation boundary (2026-11-12)

New mounted Material/Cupertino tests verify that a schema-based `SHOW` rule reads data under `NoValidation`, unaffected by a host additional-error change, while a schema `if`/`then` condition without a UI rule leaves its Control visible. The first JVM run exposed a Material defect: the shared input rendered even when resolved `visible` was false. `MaterialInputControl` now returns before composing a hidden field. All four focused tests pass on JVM and iOS simulator, and the full Compose JVM suite passes 884 tests with zero failures or skips. The first iOS link ran out of disk during debug-symbol generation; removing generated simulator binaries restored space and the rerun passed all four methods. Two §15.2 paragraphs are classified into three requirement rows; 78 keyword candidates await review. Pending-work isolation, host valid-command guards, `else`/dependency conditions and JS/Wasm/Android interaction remain open.

## Temporal picker disposal on rule hide (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino date, time and date-time controls now return at the renderer boundary when their resolved visibility is false. This unmounts an open picker dialog along with the shared input when a rule hides the control. Six mounted tests open each picker, hide its control through a live rule-data change, and verify both the picker action and Cancel dialog disappear without writing form data. Two more mounted tests enter an invalid date draft, observe pending validity while the data rule still shows the field, then hide the control and verify validity becomes valid without a form write. All eight pass on JVM and iOS simulator. The full Compose JVM suite passed 890 tests with zero failures or skips before the two new tests were added; those two passed in focused runs on both platforms. JS, Wasm and Android interaction remain open.

## Host command validity guard (2026-11-12)

A mounted host Submit button now demonstrates the §15.2 combined-validity integration. It enables only when `onValidityChange` reports `Valid`; a server additional error, a renderer's pending invalid date draft, a schema date-bound error, and `NoValidation` each disable the command. Clearing or resolving each condition re-enables it without an unintended submit. Paired Material/Cupertino tests pass on JVM and iOS simulator, two tests per platform with zero failures or skips. The date-bound failure was verified as a schema error; the server error was verified as an additional error. JS, Wasm and Android host interaction remain open.

## Conditional validation without inferred presentation (2026-11-12)

Four more mounted Material/Cupertino cases exercise `else` and `dependentRequired`: each keyword makes the document invalid when its condition applies, and resolving it restores validity, while the name control stays rendered throughout. Together with the existing rule and `if`/`then` cases, all eight `ConditionalRuleBoundaryUiTest` methods pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips. The first JS browser build ran out of disk during test compilation; generated browser caches were removed, and the retry completed successfully. Android interaction and other dependency dialects remain open.

## Property dependency error routing and clearing (2026-11-12)

The shared validator adapter previously took the final schema-path segment as the error keyword, so array-valued draft-07 `dependencies` and modern `dependentRequired` errors were mislabeled with the triggering property name. It now keeps the enclosing dependency keyword and still extracts the missing property for control-path routing; schema-valued `dependencies` and `dependentSchemas` continue to report their nested `required` keyword. Three shared core tests cover absent, present and empty triggers, directionality, whole-object schema application and missing-control paths on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. Two mounted Material/Cupertino tests place the missing billing-address error on its input, clear it after host data adds the address, and confirm that clearing the purchase-order control removes only its key. Those UI tests pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Full JVM suites pass 435 core tests (13 existing skips) and 900 Compose example tests (zero skips), with no failures. §15.9's dependency paragraph is classified; object-level feedback without a rendered control, hidden-field feedback, browser UI and Android interaction remain open.

## Omitted dependency feedback in Groups (2026-11-12)

The shared `ContainerErrorIndex` now indexes direct missing-property dependency errors by their triggering property. A Group with an enabled validation indicator can count a missing dependent property even when its control is omitted from the authored layout; an included or rule-hidden dependent control continues through the normal scope count, so the error is counted once. The index uses complete data-path segments and honors ValidateAndHide. Material and Cupertino Group renderers consume the shared count. Four new mounted cases cover omitted and rule-hidden billing controls, preserving invalid form validity and clearing the marker when the host supplies the address. All six `PropertyDependencyUiTest` cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with zero failures or skips. Full JVM suites pass 436 core tests (13 existing skips) and 904 Compose example tests (zero skips). The focused `ContainerErrorIndexTest` suite passes 11 cases on iOS, JS and Wasm. A Group without an enabled indicator and a headerless layout still lack this object-level message; Android interaction remains open.

Material and Cupertino Groups now also render a translated direct dependency message above their fields when the dependent control was omitted, independently of the optional validation marker. This keeps the explicit marker opt-out intact while making the missing field discoverable in an expanded Group. Two more mounted cases verify the direct text, its disappearance after the host supplies the value, and no marker when disabled. All eight `PropertyDependencyUiTest` methods pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips; the full JVM suites pass 436 core and 906 Compose example tests, with the same 13 existing core skips. Headerless layouts and collapsed Groups with their marker disabled still need object-level feedback; Android interaction remains open.

Collapsed Material and Cupertino Groups now keep the same direct missing-dependency message visible below their header while their fields are closed, even when the optional validation marker is disabled. Two new mounted tests verify the initial closed state, message removal after the host supplies the dependent value, and reopening without a data edit. All ten `PropertyDependencyUiTest` cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips; the full Compose JVM suite passes 908 tests. Both updated Group renderers compile for JS, while the two new collapsed cases lack a JS browser interaction run because generated bundles exceed current disk headroom. Headerless layouts and Android interaction remain open.

Headerless Material and Cupertino VerticalLayout and HorizontalLayout now show a translated direct dependency error when the dependent Control is absent from the authored layout. Each layout handles only its direct Controls; a nested Group owns its own message, so the error is not repeated. Six mounted cases cover both layout directions, clearing after the host supplies the missing value, and nested Group ownership. All 16 `PropertyDependencyUiTest` cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips. The full JVM suites pass 437 core tests (13 existing skips) and 914 Compose example tests (zero skips). The first iOS native link failed with less than 300 MiB free disk; after deleting generated JS dependencies, the same focused test passed.
