The first JS browser link also ran out of disk after compilation; after deleting generated webcomponent bundles, the same focused browser test passed. Android interaction and broader dependency configurations remain open.

A direct dependency trigger in a headerless layout can have its dependent Control in a nested Group. The parent now checks all authored descendant validation scopes before deciding that the target is unrepresented, while still handling only its direct triggers. Two mounted Material/Cupertino cases verify one inline error, no duplicate parent message, and validity recovery when host data supplies the missing value. All 18 focused dependency UI cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips. The full JVM suites pass 437 core tests (13 existing skips) and 916 Compose example tests (zero skips). The JS browser executable needed `-Pkotlin.incremental.js.ir=false` to stay within the available disk space; the first two incremental-cache attempts exhausted it. Android interaction and broader dependency configurations remain open.

The validator emits one grouped error when a property dependency or `required` schema names several missing fields. The adapter now uses the declared schema array and current instance object to expand that error into one routed error per absent name; it no longer depends on splitting a human-readable message to identify every property. Core tests cover draft-07 `dependencies`, 2020-12 `dependentRequired`, schema-valued dependencies, progressive fulfillment and property names containing a comma or slash. Two mounted Material/Cupertino cases show two inline errors, then one, then none as host data supplies each dependent field. Focused core tests pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS Node and Wasm browser; all 20 dependency UI tests pass on JVM and iOS. Full JVM suites pass 440 core tests (13 existing skips) and 918 Compose example tests (zero skips). The JS UI test sources compile, but the browser runner started without writing a test result and was stopped after repeated live polls; two attempts included one case run in isolation. Wasm UI test sources compile, but executable cache generation ran out of local disk in two attempts. The earlier 18 UI cases retain their JS/Wasm browser passes; the two new cases still need those target runs.

## Empty JSON Pointer segments (2026-11-12)

`JsonPointer.resolve` previously treated `/` and `#/` as root and dropped empty segments inside longer pointers. It now distinguishes the root pointer from an empty-name property at any depth. Focused tests cover `/`, `#/`, `//name` and `//`, a rule condition that must read a field beneath the empty-name property instead of a same-named root field, and clearing only the host error for that edited value. The three affected test classes pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS Node and Wasm browser; the full JVM suites pass 443 core tests (13 existing skips) and 918 Compose example tests (zero skips). This fixes pointer resolution for these paths; broader dot-path representation and mounted UI behavior for literal empty or dot-containing keys remain to verify.

## Restricted array recovery (2026-11-12)

Four mounted Material/Cupertino multi-select cases start with imported arrays above `maxItems` or below `minItems` under explicit `restrict: true`. Mounting leaves invalid data intact. Over-maximum arrays allow removal toward validity while addition remains disabled; under-minimum arrays allow each addition toward validity while removal remains disabled. The existing shared resulting-count policy already implements these transitions, so this checkpoint adds regression evidence and corrects a stale §15.8 ledger claim that multi-select/chips are unimplemented. All 16 `MultiSelectUiTest` cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator, and the full Compose JVM suite passes 922 tests with zero failures or skips. JS test sources compile, but the browser executable sync ran out of local disk before the four new cases could execute. Wasm and Android interaction remain open. Candidate paragraph at spec line 2766 is classified normative and mapped to S15.8-04; the candidate checker reports 76 of 180 paragraphs awaiting review.

At `minItems = maxItems = 1`, the multi-select's Add and Remove actions are both disabled, so the earlier UI had no way to change a selected value. Both Material and Cupertino now expose Replace for finite choices in that fixed-count state. A shared updater changes only the still-matching occurrence, rejects a value already selected elsewhere under `uniqueItems`, and leaves the count unchanged. The action buttons stack beside the selected label so Replace remains clickable in a 320 dp form. The menu closes when host data changes; the action label updates live in Bulgarian and returns to English for an unsupported German tag. Two mounted cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator, and the focused helper test passes on JVM, iOS simulator, JS Node and Wasm browser. Updated JS and Wasm UI test sources compile, but browser interaction for the new cases remains unverified. The first post-locale iOS link failed with low disk space; deleting generated Wasm packages and the incomplete binary let the focused retry pass. Full JVM suites pass 444 core tests (13 existing skips) and 924 Compose example tests (zero skips).

The fixed-count Replace test now opens the menu before changing the host to read-only. Both Material and Cupertino remove the action and pending option without emitting a data change, then restore the action when read-only clears. The same tests open the menu before a DISABLE rule becomes true, confirm it closes without a replacement, and restore the action when the rule clears. The focused JVM and iOS simulator pairs pass. Spec line 2745 is classified normative in S15.8-05 as partial evidence; other mutation actions and target interactions remain to verify. Candidate review is 105 of 180 paragraphs classified, 75 pending.

Replace previously appeared only when `minItems = maxItems`. The shared Material/Cupertino finite-choice renderer now exposes it whenever a different allowed choice exists under `restrict`, even if only one count bound is currently met. Two new mounted cases swap at `maxItems = 2`, remove to `minItems = 1`, then swap again at the minimum; both preserve the count for each replacement. All 20 `MultiSelectUiTest` cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator with zero failures or skips. The shared updater still guards stale positions and duplicate targets. JS/Wasm/Android interaction and other array presentations remain open.

## Array-choice permission changes and browser gate (2026-11-12)

Material and Cupertino now clear pending Add and Replace menus when a host read-only switch or DISABLE rule makes the control noninteractive. The menus stay closed when editing resumes. Two new mounted tests open Add before each permission change, confirm no pending option or data write, then reopen and select normally; the existing fixed-count Replace tests now check that its menu does not reopen automatically. All 22 `MultiSelectUiTest` cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator, and all 14 `ChipsUiTest` cases pass on JVM.

A focused `:examples-compose:jsBrowserTest --tests '*MultiSelectUiTest*'` attempt compiled JS test sources but first failed in the unrelated webcomponent production webpack dependency of `jsProcessResources`. Excluding that resource task let the test executable compile, but both executable-sync attempts ran out of disk while copying Compose JS files and source maps, before Karma executed a test. The second attempt started with about 219 MiB free after removing generated output and the unused Wasm cache; the same error recurred. The incomplete generated JS packages were removed afterward. The new 22 cases therefore have no JS browser interaction result; Wasm and Android remain unverified.

## Additional-property rename at a count boundary (2026-11-12)

The Material and Cupertino additional-properties editors now close a pending rename when host read-only state or the backing object changes. Their Save actions check current edit permission and use a shared atomic rename updater that accepts only the object snapshot the editor opened, revalidates the proposed name, and preserves key order and value without changing the property count. It rejects a stale snapshot, a newly conflicting key and an invalid name. Two mounted cases use `minProperties = maxProperties = 2`: Add and Delete stay disabled, Rename succeeds, and read-only or host replacement cancels a draft without a data write. The new mounted pair passes on JVM and iOS simulator; all five `AdditionalPropertiesTest` cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS Node and Wasm browser, and the existing pagination rename suite passes on JVM. S15.8-06 records partial coverage. JS/Wasm/Android interaction and disabled-rule transitions remain open.

The additional-properties Add handler now rechecks the full current object count, proposed name and original object snapshot in its updater. It rejects stale and over-maximum additions under `restrict`, including when declared fields consume capacity. A paired mounted case starts below `minProperties`, adds a dynamic key to become valid, then switches local `restrict` to false and commits a third key with Invalid validity. All four focused restriction UI cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator; all six shared additional-property cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS Node and Wasm browser. The first mounted iOS link ran out of disk during `dsymutil`; deleting its incomplete binary and generated Wasm output allowed the focused retry to pass. Mounted JS/Wasm and Android interaction remain open.

## Renderer prevention inventory (2026-11-12)

The §15.8 requirement that renderer specifications declare preventive support is now classified normative and tracked as S15.8-07. `docs/restrict-prevention-matrix.md` lists the actual constrained interactions and mutation checks for ordinary/tuple arrays, checkbox groups, finite and free-entry choices, additional properties, text, numeric, temporal and file controls. It explicitly separates validation-only behavior and open evidence such as array Add reducer races, keyboard delivery, picker operation and browser/device interaction. This is partial catalog coverage, not target certification. Candidate review is 106 of 180 paragraphs classified, 74 pending; the ledger has 313 atomic rows.

The prevention inventory exposed ordinary array Add as a button/click guard without a reducer-time count check. Material table Add (also used by Cupertino) now dispatches a snapshot-guarded updater that rechecks the resulting `maxItems` count; a missing or wrong-shaped incoming array can still be repaired by Add at the helper level. Four mounted table/expandable cases on JVM and iOS simulator add from empty through the maximum, then confirm local `restrict:false` permits an over-maximum commit with Invalid validity. The two shared count-helper tests pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS Node and Wasm browser. The first combined iOS link ran out of disk during `dsymutil`; deleting its incomplete binary and generated core JS/Wasm caches let the focused mounted retry pass. Browser/Android array interaction and other array modes remain open.
The existing 12-case `ArrayActionPolicyUiTest` suite also passes on JVM after the Add updater change, covering table, expandable and list-with-detail action policy and confirmation behavior.

The authored `detail` option selects a separate expandable array-layout renderer. It now uses the same snapshot/count Add updater as the table path. Both array renderers display an imported non-array value in the header without rewriting it on mount, using English/Bulgarian text and English fallback for unsupported locale tags. They resolve the stored value before rendering, so a missing array is not mislabeled as explicit `null`; Add can repair either a missing field, explicit null or a numeric value. The 12-case mounted table/expandable class passes on JVM and iOS simulator across Material/Cupertino, with zero failures or skips. The two-case array locale bundle class passes on JVM. Browser/Android interaction and other array modes remain open.
The existing 12-case table/expandable/list-with-detail action-policy suite also passes on JVM after the expandable renderer change.

Both Material and Cupertino tuple renderers now show a stored non-array value instead of silently presenting an empty tuple. A fixed-position edit repairs it; the tail Add updater preserves the actual stored snapshot and rechecks `maxItems` at commit. The 83-case mounted `TupleUiTest` class passes on JVM and iOS simulator, including paired wrong-shape repair, English/Bulgarian locale switching, unsupported-locale English fallback and the tail maximum. The two-case shared count-helper test passes on iOS simulator and JS Node with the new non-array snapshot assertions. An initial empty-prefix fixture exposed a validator schema-compilation rejection, so the mounted repair test uses a valid one-position tuple. Browser-mounted tuple interaction remains to verify.

Tuple actions, position labels, invalid-value/configuration text and delete confirmation now use a shared English/Bulgarian renderer bundle. The host translator receives the localized default and can override it; unsupported `de-DE` remains English fallback. The full 85-case mounted tuple class passes on JVM and iOS simulator, including two new paired no-translator Bulgarian action/dialog cases. The two-case core bundle test passes on JVM and iOS simulator. The first iOS link exhausted disk (`errno=28`); clearing generated JS output and native test binaries let the identical focused retry pass. Browser-mounted interaction remains open.

The §18.3 initial-focus clause is now classified normative and mapped as S18.3-01. Shared Material/Cupertino text inputs request focus once when `options.focus` is true and the input mounts visible and enabled. Eight mounted cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator; host data, locale and option rerenders do not reclaim focus after the user moves it, local false overrides global focus, and string `"true"` does not request focus. Read-only remains focusable only with the existing `separateReadonlyFromDisabled` mode; the default host read-only path disables the control. Multiple-focus diagnostics, other controls, remount/disabled scenarios and browser/Android interaction remain open.

The §18.3 required-marker clause is now mapped as S18.3-02. Shared Material/Cupertino text inputs expose localized `Required field`/`Задължително поле` as the input's state description while validation stays Invalid; hiding the asterisk leaves that state intact. Material now displays its required marker by default, and both inputs require a real JSON boolean for the hide option. The two-case mounted class and ten-case core locale class pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Other control families and accessible help/error associations remain open.

The §18.3 description clause is now mapped as S18.3-03. Material and Cupertino text inputs retain visible help when an inline error is present, and Cupertino respects `showUnfocusedDescription` instead of using the description as an unfocused placeholder. Paired mounted tests pass on JVM and iOS simulator: true shows help beside a semantic error, false and string `"true"` hide it while unfocused, and focus reveals it again. The input semantics carry visible help in state description and the error in its error property; the focus and required-marker regression classes also pass on JVM/iOS. Direct screen-reader behavior remains unverified.

Six previously unreviewed keyword paragraphs in §§14, 16 and 17 are classified. Function-valued Button scripts are an optional nonwire host feature; Kotlin currently supports the JSON string path and host action callback, so the conditional event-binding and no-stringification rules remain explicit adoption gates. Adaptive small-screen semantics and content/container layout are partial, backed by the existing mounted sizing and presentation-selection evidence; alternate host UI schemas are outside the portable element language. Service authorization is an external MUST and is not verified by renderer visibility; README now states the integration boundary. The ledger adds five rows and the candidate checker reports 66 paragraphs awaiting classification.

Four §18.1 renderer-behavior paragraphs are now classified and mapped as separate documentation requirements. The new partial catalog records Material/Cupertino string and tuple selection, provenance, option effects, invalid data, family distinctions, and selecting/incompatible examples; it links the AG Grid, Monaco and TemplateLayout browser-only fallback review without claiming those engines. The registry inventory remains a list, not a behavioral certification. Remaining renderer and cell entries, per-option provenance exceptions and target evidence keep §18.1 partial; the candidate checker reports 62 paragraphs awaiting classification.

The combined §18.2a `views` paragraph is classified and split into storage and picker-presentation rows. Six new mounted typed-edit cases with restrictive date/time/date-time views pass on JVM and iOS simulator, showing that `views` does not alter configured save formats. The first iOS link exhausted disk during LLVM output; clearing generated test binaries/resources and core binaries let the identical focused retry pass. Material/Cupertino native pickers currently ignore `views`, so panel/column selection and the rule for an array with no applicable views remain unsupported; the partial behavior catalog documents this data-preserving fallback. Candidate review is now 119 of 180 classified, 61 pending.

Three §18.6 detail-selection and cycle-guard paragraphs are now classified as partial. The new paired Material/Cupertino mounted tests confirm that a generated detail for a missing object leaves the data untouched on mount and creates the object only after an edit. All 12 `DetailModeUiTest` cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator, including inline, registered, generated, array, list and explicit-table selection. Existing `ObjectDetailCycleUiTest` covers self-matching registry fallback on JVM. Array/list missing-data cases, registry rank/tie behavior and wider target interaction remain open. Candidate review is 122 of 180 classified, 58 pending; the ledger has 330 atomic rows.

The §18.6 registry-tester clause is now classified partial. Two new core tests verify zero rank acceptance, first-entry tie selection, NOT_APPLICABLE rejection and item-path context; the focused `UISchemasTest` passes on JVM and iOS simulator. Kotlin's tester type returns an `Int` synchronously, while tester purity and invocation-count independence remain caller obligations. Candidate review is 123 of 180 classified, 57 pending; the ledger has 331 atomic rows.

The remaining §18.6 relative-scope/context paragraph is classified partial. `ArrayDetailRegistryPathUiTest` now runs with both direct item schemas and item `$ref` pointers into root `$defs`. All four Material/Cupertino cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator: the registry selects `rows.1`, and its relative `name` control changes only that row. UI-schema tester and renderer tester signatures are separate in core. Nested reference variants and other targets remain open. Candidate review is 124 of 180 classified, 56 pending; the ledger has 332 atomic rows.

The §18.7 array Add initializer now recursively copies explicit JSON object/array defaults before returning them. Core tests show a new nested container for each call, schema-default identity separation, and preservation of explicit false, zero and null. Four mounted Material/Cupertino table/expandable cases start with an existing row, make no write on mount, then append two defaulted rows while preserving the original and schema. Focused core and mounted suites pass on JVM and iOS simulator. S18.7-01 and -02 track partial coverage; temporal initialization, restriction/combinator edge cases and other targets remain. Candidate review is 125 of 180 classified, 55 pending; the ledger has 334 atomic rows.

The §18.8 object editor now displays translated exact-path errors before its delegated detail layout in both Material and Cupertino, with Compose Error semantics. A registered label-only detail receives an object-level host error beside the editor even though there is no matching input; clearing the error removes the feedback without a data change. Both mounted cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator; the existing detail-mode, object-cycle and group-item-context suites pass together on JVM after the layout change. S18.8-01 records partial coverage; hidden and unrepresented descendant errors, schema-generated object errors and direct screen-reader behavior remain open. The ledger has 335 atomic rows.

The §18.8 object editor now also displays descendant errors that no control in its selected detail form can represent. The shared error index filters by segment-aware item path and authored binding scope; visible-scope collection excludes rule-hidden detail elements. Six mounted Material/Cupertino cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator: label-only detail exposes both its direct and omitted-property errors, bound controls keep their error at the field without a duplicate object message, and a hidden field exposes its error beside the object until the host makes the field visible. The pure index test passes on both targets; dependency, group-item-context, detail-mode and object-cycle suites also pass together on JVM. S18.8-01 and -02 remain partial for nested rule contexts, validation modes and physical screen-reader checks. The ledger has 336 atomic rows.

Two §18.9 paragraphs are classified partial. Array-own error text in expandable, table and list-with-detail presentations now has Compose Error semantics. Six new mounted empty-array `minItems` cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator with child summaries hidden; the existing six-case `ArrayValidationSummaryUiTest` checks indicator/count/locale changes and own-array error preservation, for 12 passing cases total. Existing core index tests separate `rows.1` from `rows.10`, exclude array-own errors from item groups and handle mapped host and escaped paths; existing row-detail UI tests cover item badge names and navigation. Reorder/deletion remapping and direct screen-reader behavior remain open. Candidate review is 127 of 180 classified, 53 pending; the ledger has 338 atomic rows.

§18.10 `contains` now has direct core and mounted evidence. Four core cases pass on JVM/iOS for the default one-match rule, match counts rather than array length, inert min/max count keywords without contains, and tuple prefix/tail independence. Six Material/Cupertino expandable/table/list mounted cases pass on JVM/iOS: an empty array exposes its own accessible `contains` error even with child summaries hidden, and host replacement with a matching item clears it without a form write. S18.10-01 is partial. S18.10-02 explicitly records unsupported restricted deletion: the current Delete guards check minItems but do not preserve a satisfied contains minimum. Candidate review is 128 of 180 classified, 52 pending; the ledger has 340 atomic rows.

The §18.10 restricted Delete gap is now partially implemented. A shared helper validates current root data and a hypothetical post-delete root through the compiled validator, then blocks the discrete deletion only if an array-path `contains`/`minContains` failure is newly introduced. Expandable, table and list-with-detail Delete buttons, requests, pending confirmations and commit handlers use the same per-item check when `restrict` is true. Two core helper tests and all 12 mounted contains cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator; existing array count, action policy, confirmation and list identity suites pass together on JVM. Two iOS links exhausted disk; clearing generated native binaries plus unrelated generated examples/web outputs let the identical focused retry pass. Tuple/tail deletion, long-array cost, pending host races and other targets remain open. S18.10-02 is partial.

§18.10 restricted tuple tail Delete now uses the same current/result validator check in both Material and Cupertino. A guard instance caches the unchanged-root validation per rendered array, while each possible removal validates its own result; a core counting test checks one baseline validation for two candidate rows. Paired tuple cases keep the last matching tail item disabled, then permit deleting a nonmatching tail after host replacement and confirmation. All 87 `TupleUiTest` cases, all 12 `ContainsArrayUiTest` cases and all three `ContainsRemovalTest` cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator. S18.10-02 remains partial for other mutation paths/targets, stale host races and performance measurement.

Eight mounted stale-confirmation cases now cover the §18.10 guard in expandable, table, list-with-detail and tuple tail Delete for Material/Cupertino. Each opens confirmation for a nonmatching row, replaces host data so the same index becomes the only match, and verifies that the dialog closes, Delete disables and no form write occurs. The full `ContainsArrayUiTest` (18 cases) and `TupleUiTest` (89 cases) pass on JVM and iOS simulator. S18.10-02 remains partial for other mutation paths/targets, measured validation cost and custom validator behavior.

The §18.13 Label summary path now omits Edit and Clear for a property-bound object cell without explicit `detail`, matching its presentation-only contract. The same path already omits actions on a row-bound virtual column; existing explicit-detail tests still exercise Edit and scoped validation. Six `ArrayCompositeCellDialogUiTest` cases and ten `ArrayCompositeCellValidationUiTest` cases pass in both JVM and iOS simulator focused runs, with no failures. AG Grid summary behavior, browser/device interaction and broader composite-cell coverage remain open.

§18.13 generated composite summaries now distinguish stored scalar previews from generated descriptions. The core helper returns text plus a generated flag; Material/Cupertino shared table presentation renders generated text in secondary italic type. A nonempty object whose selected Control field is absent, null, blank or not scalar uses the localized field label with `composite.summary.unspecified`; empty objects and arrays retain their distinct descriptions. Bulgarian bundles cover the composite summary keys, while English remains the fallback, and form translators may override either. Four core preview cases and eight mounted dialog cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator; the mounted cases switch locale and replace values without editing. Core and Material JS/Wasm compilation passes. Physical theme contrast, browser interaction and fuller summary-label/schema-reference resolution remain open.

§18.13 composite object/array type markers now resolve cell `showTypeIndicator`, collection option and top-level config in that order, with explicit false suppressing a true default. A marker appears beside the summary only for an actual object or array and is absent from accessible text semantics; scalar and missing values have none. Five core and ten mounted Material/Cupertino composite-cell cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator, and core/Material JS and Wasm compilation passes. S18.13-01 is partial for AG Grid hosting, browser/device interaction, theme contrast and additional option-placement scenarios. The existing browser-only integration review still requires a proven disposable DOM host before AG Grid, Monaco or TemplateLayout can be claimed in the webcomponent; portable fallbacks and explicit diagnostics remain.

§18.13 composite table-cell dialogs now resolve `showEmptyButton`, `showRemoveButton` and four action-label overrides from the selected cell, collection and top-level config. Default English/Bulgarian action labels are bundled; authored labels are translated as keys. Clear empties an object/array draft, Remove unsets a property draft, and both remain private until Apply. A revision key disposes the prior child editor so a queued edit cannot restore a cleared draft. The Remove action is absent for a row-bound cell and for a required property under `restrict`; a nonempty accepted removal still uses the shared confirmation policy. Two core option cases, three Material locale cases and 14 mounted Material/Cupertino dialog cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator. The full mounted JVM suite passes 1016 tests with no failures; core/Material JS and Wasm compilation passes. S18.13-02 is partial for Apply/Cancel tooltips, other restrict constraints, nested pending dialogs, physical focus and browser interaction.

The §18.13 restrict clause corrects the earlier parent-required assumption: ordinary required values remain clearable/removable under the shared clear contract, while protected child fields and structural minima prevent Empty when `restrict` is true. `clearable: false` now hides the external cell clear action and both optional dialog actions. A new core case covers required children, `minProperties`, `minItems` and unrestricted emptying; four additional mounted Material/Cupertino cases cover parent-required removal, protected-child Empty suppression and local clearable false. The focused JVM core and 18-case dialog suites pass. The earlier iOS and full JVM evidence predates this correction and is not used to claim final target coverage.

The final §18.13 restriction correction passes three focused core tests and 18 mounted Material/Cupertino dialog tests on JVM/iOS, plus core/Material JS/Wasm compilation. The full mounted JVM suite passes 1020 tests with no failures. Dialog action options now resolve from the current cell/collection/config on every composition rather than being frozen when opened; two more mounted cases per family revoke and restore global `clearable` while the dialog remains open. All 20 focused dialog cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator after that change, and Material JS/Wasm recompiles. Wider restrict constraints, deferred Apply flushing, Apply/Cancel tooltips and browser/device interactions remain open.

Background scalar table cells now receive disabled renderer props while a composite-cell or row-detail dialog is open; the draft editor remains enabled and background editing returns after Cancel. The 20 composite-cell and eight row-detail mounted cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator with zero failures. Material JS and Wasm source compilation passes. A broader examples-compose JS/Wasm test-source build exhausted disk while compiling the webcomponent production JS executable; no browser test result is claimed from that attempt. A source audit confirms that shipped renderer bundles, demo choices and imported example translations contain only English and Bulgarian. Unsupported German tags are retained only as negative English-fallback tests, and the plan now states the two-locale scope. The three spec inventory/coverage scripts and `git diff --check` pass; 52 normative paragraphs still await classification.

The §18.13 composite-cell footer now wraps Apply and Cancel in localized tooltip components in both Material and Cupertino. The English defaults are the spec text; Bulgarian defaults use the existing renderer bundle, with host translator override through the shared resolver. The four-case bundle test and 20 composite-cell plus eight row-detail mounted tests pass on JVM; all pass on iOS simulator after a low-disk link retry that cleared generated build output. Material and Cupertino JS/Wasm source compilation passes. A proposed desktop pointer test could not observe either tooltip after hovering the footer inside the separate dialog popup, so it was removed rather than counted as passing evidence. Direct hover/focus and browser/device interaction remain unverified; S18.13-02 stays partial.

Modal isolation for §18.13 now covers background actions as well as scalar cells. While a composite-cell or row-detail draft is open, table Add/Delete/Move/Edit and external clear controls, panel selection and pagination are disabled; their handlers also recheck the dialog state. Apply, Cancel and the draft editor remain active, and background actions return after Cancel. Assertions in both Material and Cupertino dialog scenarios verify the background action state. All 20 composite-cell and eight row-detail focused cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator; the changed Material renderer compiles on JS/Wasm. The first JVM assertion matched a nested array's identically named action and was narrowed to the background action; the final run has zero failures. Wider modal focus/keyboard traversal and browser/device interaction remain unverified.

§18.4 pending-action handling now guards extended Button dispatch. A pending renderer-owned edit disables the action with English/Bulgarian state text; the click handler and coroutine start both recheck pending state, and the action event reads the controller's current form snapshot at execution. Two new paired Material/Cupertino cases in `ButtonActionUiTest` prove an incomplete date draft does not dispatch its older committed value, then a corrected edit dispatches once with the new date. The 16-case focused Button class passes on JVM and iOS simulator; a three-case locale bundle class passes on JVM and confirms English/Bulgarian plus unsupported-language fallback. Extended-renderers JS/Wasm main source compiles. Generic flush for committable debounced edits and other data-consuming actions remain open. Candidate review classified the four §18.3/18.4 paragraphs as normative and the five Choice cards paragraphs as proposals outside v1 conformance, reducing pending review from 52 to 43. An exploratory Choice cards resolver and its JVM tests were removed after the proposal status was confirmed; a JS browser test process that had not reported completion was interrupted as it no longer covered active code. No JS/Wasm runtime result is claimed from it.

§18.25 cron now has a shared six-field format validator registered with the JSON Schema host and a Material/Cupertino string control with raw text, period-first picker, staged Apply/Cancel and immediate `showActions: false`. The validator rejects five/seven fields and `@reboot`, accepts the seven named aliases and numeric/name/day-selector forms, and exposes errors through schema validation without additional-error publication. Four core cases and three picker cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Eight mounted cases in both families pass on JVM and in a focused iOS simulator run, including untouched alias preservation, Cancel, edited Apply, invalid direct text, immediate picker edits, Clear, read-only guards and live Bulgarian labels. Core and extended renderer JS/Wasm main sources compile. The earlier full iOS examples suite ran 1,139 cases with one unrelated `ArrayChoicesFixtureUiTest.materialImportedChoicesAndTokens` failure because the `Heavy` node was absent; all six cron cases then present passed. The final focused eight-case cron iOS rerun passed. `check-spec-candidates` reports 180 paragraphs with 28 pending; `check-spec-coverage` reports 49 examples, 46 imported and three gaps. Browser interaction, disabled/host-replacement cases and complex selector fidelity remain open.

Cron follow-up: a disable rule now discards an open staged picker draft, so reenabling cannot reveal a stale dialog; host data replacement also closes the picker without a write. Invalid picker fields retain their draft, show feedback and disable Apply while Cancel preserves the stored value. The pure picker helper no longer pins lower wildcard time fields for an invalid upper-field draft. Four validator, three picker and fourteen mounted Material/Cupertino cases pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Extended renderer JS/Wasm main compilation, the spec candidate and example coverage checks, and `git diff --check` pass. Ledger rows S18.25-01 through -03 separate format validation, picker semantics and raw text/actions. Browser/device interaction, complex selector fidelity and accessible dialog behavior remain open.

Cron dialect comparison against the pinned React/Svelte helpers found two portable parser mismatches. Positive step syntax is now digit-validated and bounded only for iteration, so `0/999999999999999999999` selects its start instead of failing `Int` conversion; `+1` and zero remain invalid. Weekday `7` is normalized to Sunday before range/step expansion, so `7/2` selects Sunday, Tuesday, Thursday and Saturday, while `FRI-7` remains valid raw wrapping syntax. `L-0` follows the reference offset acceptance. The four-case `CronFormatTest` suite passes on JVM, iOS simulator, JS Node and Wasm browser, each with zero failures or skips. `check-spec-candidates`, `check-spec-coverage` and `git diff --check` pass. UI behavior for the changed dialect inputs remains covered by the existing raw-text path but has no new mounted scenario.

The fourteen mounted `CronControlUiTest` cases now pass in both JS and Wasm browser test runners with zero failures or skips, matching the earlier JVM/iOS result. The first JS attempt compiled its large test bundle and reached `jsBrowserTest` but produced no result for several minutes; it was interrupted. Reusing that compiled bundle with Gradle info logging completed in 15 seconds, connected ChromeHeadless and produced the fourteen-case XML. Generated JS test bundles and caches were removed to free space before the focused Wasm run, which passed in 66 seconds. These are Compose browser test-harness interactions, not a real webcomponent host test. The remaining cron gates are complex selector fidelity, picker accessibility/focus, physical device behavior and actual webcomponent integration.

Cron period buttons now publish selected semantics in both Material and Cupertino. The active period also publishes a state description through the English/Bulgarian renderer bundle. Two new mounted cases verify that selecting Hour moves the selected state from Day; the live Bulgarian locale cases verify the selected-period description. The focused 16-case class passes on JVM and iOS simulator. Extended renderer JS/Wasm main source and examples-compose JS/Wasm test sources compile. Browser runtime for these two new cases is still being checked; the earlier fourteen cases passed in both browser runners. Direct screen-reader behavior, focus order and physical keyboard/device interaction remain unverified.

The final 16-case cron class passes with zero failures or skips on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. The first JS browser attempt rebuilt its test executable and then remained in Karma webpack preparation without launching Chrome; after five minutes with no new result, it was interrupted. Reusing the compiled bundle in a focused retry connected ChromeHeadless and passed all 16 cases in 16 seconds. Generated JS output was cleared before the Wasm browser run, which passed all 16 cases in 52 seconds. This verifies Compose test-harness semantics and interaction on browser targets; real webcomponent hosting, direct screen-reader speech and physical keyboard/device behavior are separate remaining gates.

## Color profile codec and mounted controls (2026-11-13)

The shared color codec now lives in core. It parses `#RGB`, `#RRGGBB`, `#RRGGBBAA`, comma-separated RGB/RGBA, HSL/HSLA and HSB/HSBA; it rejects unsupported values without inventing a preview color. Schema `format: color` uses that same parser and reports a normal `format` error; UI-only selection does not install a schema constraint. Serialization supports `hex`, `hex3`, `rgb` and `hsb`, including alpha, with transparent `hex3` refused. Material and Cupertino preset, channel and alpha edits use the configured save format. The control honors `colorTextEntry: false`, keeps a Bulgarian/English Clear action when permitted, shows invalid imported text, uses mapped readonly state and lays out its controls vertically so taps do not overlap. One core schema-format test passes on JVM/iOS/JS browser/Wasm browser; three pure codec/channel tests pass on JVM/iOS. Twenty-two mounted Material/Cupertino cases pass on JVM/iOS/JS browser/Wasm browser with zero failures/skips: initial view, non-mutating switch, reopen reset, host/readonly invalidation, transparent hex3 refusal, incomplete direct-text draft with pending validity, HSL normalization, invalid imported value without a pending edit, empty picker Hex entry and saved slider edits. The full JVM regressions pass: core 476 tests (13 existing skips), extended renderers 351, Compose examples 1186, with zero failures; the final picker-only feedback edit reran the focused class on all four targets. Android main compilation passes for core, extended renderers and Compose examples after normal dependency resolution; the offline attempt lacked `kotlinx-coroutines-android:1.11.0`. The JS browser bundle first exhausted disk during sync. Later cold Karma runs stayed silent; keeping the synced bundle, removing its compiler cache and excluding redundant production/compile tasks produced the 22-case JS XML with zero failures. Native Cupertino picker styling, physical keyboard/screen-reader checks, Android device and actual webcomponent interaction remain open.

## Native Cupertino color picker composition (2026-11-13)

The color picker now uses Cupertino buttons for opening, clearing, presets and channel views, a filled Cupertino button for the selected view, Cupertino sliders for channels and alpha, and Cupertino input fields for Hex/RGB/HSB/alpha values. `CupertinoInputControl` accepts an optional modifier on its underlying field so picker channel inputs keep accessible names without replacing the shared native input implementation. The existing 22 mounted color cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser after this change, with zero failures or skips. The first JS executable sync ran out of disk. Compiling the executable, deleting its separate compiler cache, syncing the retained output and running Karma as separate tasks produced the 22-case JS XML; generated JS bundles were cleaned afterward. Android main compilation passes. Full JVM suites pass with zero failures or skips: Cupertino 5, extended renderers 351, Compose examples 1186. Verify selected-view appearance across light/dark themes, physical keyboard and screen-reader behavior, Android device interaction and actual webcomponent hosting.

## Conditional object-field projection (2026-11-13)

The opt-in object projection now supplies active properties and required markers to generated and authored details while leaving the original schema with document validation. It walks `allOf`, `if`/`then`/`else`, `dependencies`, `dependentSchemas` and `dependentRequired` at each rendered object boundary, and reserves inactive branch properties from additional-property editing. An invalid condition produces a visible diagnostic and does not select `else`. Four core JVM cases cover opt-in precedence, nested `allOf`, overlapping property constraints, null-valued dependency triggers, required-only dependencies and failed condition compilation. Four mounted Material/Cupertino JVM fixture cases cover generated and authored placement, live business/personal branch changes, purchase-order dependency removal, retained inactive data and no incidental writes. Full affected JVM suites pass: core 489 tests (13 existing skips), Material 77, Cupertino five and Compose examples 1340, with zero failures. Core and both renderer families compile for JS and Wasm; the first cross-target attempt caught a JVM-only map call, which was replaced and recompiled successfully. Browser/device interaction, named-template placement, remote references and deeper conditional compositions remain to verify.

## Conditional fields missing-object and browser follow-up (2026-11-13)

Opted-in object details now project against an empty object when the bound value is missing, so a generated form still shows the eligible `else` fields without creating data. JSON Schema boolean `if` conditions select their appropriate branches. The projection also resolves local `$defs` references from the original root. Visible projection diagnostics use the renderer translation path: Bulgarian and regional Bulgarian use the Bulgarian message; unsupported locales, including German tags, use English. Eight mounted Material/Cupertino JVM fixture cases pass, including missing-object mount, host replacement with zero incidental writes and visible Bulgarian invalid-condition feedback. Seven core conditional tests pass on JVM, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips; both renderer families compile for JS and Wasm. Browser-mounted object interaction, iOS simulator and Android device behavior remain to verify.

## Conditional retained-property reservation (2026-11-13)

The active projection keeps inactive branch fields out of controls, while the reserved projection classifies a retained inactive key as declared for the additional-property editor. A core test contrasts the active and reserved key lists. Two mounted Material/Cupertino JVM cases use the imported conditional fixture plus a truly dynamic `note` property: after a business-to-personal host replacement, the VAT field disappears from the form and additional editor, `note` remains editable, VAT data remains present, and no callback writes occur. All eight focused core cases pass on JVM, JS browser and Wasm browser; all ten mounted fixture cases pass on JVM. A focused mounted JS browser run compiled tests but exhausted disk during executable cache generation before Chrome started. The incomplete generated cache was removed. Retrying with `-Pkotlin.incremental.js.ir=false` compiled and synced the browser bundle within available disk, but Karma remained silent with no XML; a browser-only retry using the retained bundle also stayed silent. Both live tasks were interrupted after observation. The generated sync copy was removed to recover space. Browser interaction is still unverified.

## Conditional allOf object dispatch and overlapping editor constraints (2026-11-14)

A mounted three-way overlap exposed a real dispatch gap: a root object with `allOf` selected the higher-ranked AllOf renderer, bypassing opted-in conditional object projection. Its shared string editor accepted an edit past an active branch `maxLength`. Material and Cupertino object testers now outrank AllOf only for opted-in object Controls with `allOf`; `anyOf` and `oneOf` retain their alternative selection. The projection keeps every authored constraint in `allOf` and exposes unambiguous type/format, strictest string length bounds and any read-only marker for renderer selection and restrictive input. The paired mounted regression confirms the editor remains available and rejects the overlong edit. A rank test checks the alternative boundary.

The focused nine core and 13 mounted conditional cases pass on JVM, iOS arm64 simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips. Full affected JVM suites pass: core 494 tests (13 existing skips), Material 77, Cupertino five and Compose examples 1349, with zero failures. Core and both renderer families compile for JS/Wasm; core, both families and Compose examples compile for Android main. The mounted JS run produced ChromeHeadless XML despite webpack `os`/`path` resolution warnings. Android device interaction, named-template placement, remote references, contradictory constraints and deeper compositions remain to verify.

## Versioned durable demo preferences (2026-11-14)

`DemoPreferences` now stores durable mode, English/Bulgarian locale, demo layout, UI family and Material/Cupertino theme presets in one renderer-scoped JSON snapshot with schema version 1. It reads legacy individual keys only when the snapshot is absent, then writes the snapshot on the next save. A malformed or future-version snapshot falls back to defaults rather than reviving stale legacy values; unknown enum values fall back per field, including a retired German value. Reads and writes are guarded so unavailable storage cannot prevent default settings or form rendering. Direction, validation, read-only, generic JSON Forms options and view state stay in session and are excluded from the snapshot. The shared preference tests cover version, durable-only fields, round trip, legacy migration, corruption, future version, unknown values and blocked reads/writes. Full examples-compose suites pass 1,469 JVM, 1,443 iOS simulator, 1,442 JS browser and 1,445 Wasm browser tests with zero failures or skips. Mounted Chrome tests click the icon and find schema-path details on JS and Wasm. A Chrome Tab traversal probe did not expose the tooltip, so browser keyboard focus remains unverified. Focused JVM tests pass. The full examples-compose suites pass 1,474 JVM, 1,447 iOS simulator, 1,446 JS browser and 1,449 Wasm browser tests with no failures or skips; Android main compilation, spec example coverage (46 of 49) and whitespace checks pass. Browser/device hover and keyboard focus behavior, source/provenance links and capability notices remain open for P7.6/P7.8.

## Pinned example provenance and adaptation notices (2026-11-14)

Every built-in example now carries a pinned source URL. The generator maps all 79 upstream `@jsonforms/examples` names to one source file at commit `2c5fae62a8aace19970ac037d122fa6428cd0436`; `--sources-only` regenerates that map without rewriting time-sensitive example data. The 46 imported spec examples link to their directories at commit `7c354f4f03417c37d91080fcbd169561bce66cd6`. Local Git object checks confirm every target path exists. The adaptation catalog now records English and Bulgarian reasons for seven portable profiles, including File control and Table row details, where AG Grid categories were already removed by sync. Gradle checks the adaptation commit against the spec snapshot and embeds the notices in generated example metadata. The demo header shows source family, a localized source action and the localized notice; an unlinked host example shows a host label without an invented link. Catalog tests verify all built-ins have URLs and exactly the seven adapted fixtures have two-language notices; mounted JVM tests verify link activation and live locale changes. Mounted JS and Wasm Chrome checks activate the source action and capture the exact pinned URL while keeping the test page local. The spec sync check passes (46 selected examples). Full examples and examples-compose suites pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures/skips; examples-compose reports 1,477/1,450/1,449/1,452 tests respectively. Android main compilation, spec coverage (46 of 49 classified) and whitespace checks pass. Device link activation and broader fallback notice review remain open for P7.6.

## Shared URL host data retention (2026-11-14)

Moved the controlled URL view-state host test into common tests so the same data-retention behavior is verified on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. It repairs invalid working data through an example action, applies external form-only and Schema state, exits to the full UI, checks that the repaired data stays valid, then applies and reverses read-only view state. The test pins Material in its own settings store to run under the headless iOS UI harness, which has no native view controller for Cupertino system bars. The focused JVM test and full iOS/JS/Wasm examples-compose suites pass with zero failures. Compose data retention through actual browser Back/Forward remains open for P7.5.

## Mounted Compose data across browser history (2026-11-14)

The Chrome URL suite now changes the built-in `dynamic` example from `bla` to `aaa` with its real example action. It checks the accessible changed value, then compares a small rendered-value region in full and form-only layouts to verify that the same changed value remains after form-only navigation, Back and Forward. This rendered check is used because Chrome intermittently returns a sparse or stale accessibility tree during Compose canvas history transitions even when the captured frame shows the correct form and data. The test fixes its Chrome viewport and checks that the new value differs from the initial rendered value. The complete URL script passes 8 JS scenarios and 7 Wasm scenarios; the JS-only Web Component scenario is skipped on Wasm. This closes the single Compose working-data Back/Forward case in P7.5; broader URL interactions and dynamic accessibility behavior remain open.

## Mounted Settings, tab and drawer URL controls (2026-11-14)

Both Material and Cupertino Settings switches now give the switch its localized visible label as a Compose content description. The shared URL host UI test checks the Read-Only switch name and state. In mounted Chrome, the Settings switch writes `read-only=true`; Back and Forward restore its rendered checked state. Clicking the Data tab writes `active-tab=data`; opening the example drawer writes `drawer=true`; Back and Forward restore the visible menu. The browser suite passes 10 JS scenarios and 9 Wasm scenarios, with the JS-only Web Component case skipped on Wasm. These tests use a fixed viewport and rendered-region comparisons where Chrome exposes only sparse accessibility nodes during Compose overlay or history transitions. The focused JVM host UI test and complete iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser examples-compose suites pass with no failures; Android main compiles. Broader URL combinations and dynamic browser accessibility remain open.

## Independent Job form presentation actions (2026-11-14)

The portable Job application adaptation now replaces only the Ractive `TemplateLayout` markup with a native HorizontalLayout carrying the same three authored Button actions. Its English/Bulgarian and theme actions therefore remain available while AG Grid, Monaco and the Ractive engine stay outside the portable profile. The demo host now handles `changeLang` and `setLocale` as form-locale changes and `toggleMode` as a form-only light/dark change. The application locale/theme and form data stay unchanged; form presentation overrides are session-only and absent from the versioned preference snapshot. A nested theme scopes the form appearance, while the surrounding demo chrome retains its own theme and language.

The full-host Job test first found that the demo eagerly serialized UI-schema drafts and rejected typed `ImageView`, preventing the example from opening. The UI-schema serializer now preserves Spacer, Separator, Link, ImageView and typed element names; a round-trip test covers those fields. Mounted Material tests on all available targets and Cupertino on JVM click the actual Job buttons, verify Bulgarian/English form translation, form dark-theme state, unchanged app settings and unchanged data. A full-host test confirms English demo chrome after the Job form switches to Bulgarian. The headless iOS runner cannot mount CupertinoAppTheme without a UIViewController, so native Cupertino theme interaction remains for a device runner. Mounted Chrome loads the Job example and changes its form locale on JS and Wasm while the app toolbar and URL stay unchanged. The complete browser URL script passes 11 JS and 10 Wasm scenarios (one JS-only Web Component skip). Examples catalog suites pass 17 tests on JVM/iOS/JS/Wasm, and examples-compose iOS/JS/Wasm suites pass 1,454/1,453/1,456 tests with no failures/skips; the final full examples-compose JVM suite passes 1,481 tests with no failures/skips. Android main compilation, spec snapshot sync (46 selected), coverage (46 of 49 classified) and whitespace checks pass. Web Component form-theme/action bridging and physical-device Cupertino behavior remain open.

## Job actions through the JS Web Component (2026-11-14)

The JS-only demo embed now forwards named UI schemas, config and localized translation catalogs to the custom element. Physical-device Cupertino theme interaction remains open.

## Imported authoring-schema gate (2026-11-14)

The new read-only checker loads the pinned spec checkout's Ajv validator and registers all seven draft-07 authoring schemas by published ID. ## Mixed layout redistribution (2026-11-14)

The shared horizontal resolver already computes Fixed and full-grid Span widths before distributing Weight/Auto remainder, and the bounded vertical resolver redistributes after a weighted child's maximum. ## Unresolved Control property scope (2026-11-14)

The extended authoring schema accepts a structurally valid Control with `#/properties/missing`, but the host's schema cannot resolve it. Two mounted Material/Cupertino JVM cases parse the authored UI JSON and verify the fallback, one intact valid sibling editor, one diagnostic and no data write. The first full `:examples-compose:jvmTest` run found two tuple-position regressions; restricting this guard to property scopes preserves the tuple renderer's own invalid-position diagnostics. Shared Compose code compiles for JS, Wasm and iOS simulator, and the authoring gate passes. Non-JVM UI and other unresolved scope shapes remain open.

The paired scope tests now also replace the host schema live: the unresolved field becomes editable when its property appears and returns to the fallback when removed, with no data write. Another paired case verifies that `#/properties/a~1b` resolves a property whose name contains `/`, without a false diagnostic. Focused ControlScopeUiTest passes on JVM; its common test source compiles for JS, Wasm and iOS simulator. Browser/device interaction remains open.

Six mounted JVM cases cover object, explicit null versus missing, live locale/host replacement, zero implicit writes and repair through color selection or text entry. The full examples-compose JVM suite passes 1,722 tests with zero failures/skips; JS, Wasm and iOS simulator test sources compile. Browser/device interaction and remaining extended controls are still open for §19.

## Imported cron and null-control values (2026-11-14)

Material/Cupertino cron controls now expose imported non-string JSON through the shared stored-value status and input accessibility semantics. Null controls now display the exact imported value under an English/Bulgarian invalid-value marker, rather than saying Not set, and associate it with the checkbox error semantics. Four new paired mounted JVM cases cover an object, a number, missing data, live locale/host replacement, zero implicit writes and explicit repair. Full examples-compose JVM passes 1,724 tests and extended-renderers JVM passes 417, both with zero failures/skips. Their JS, Wasm and iOS simulator test sources compile. File controls, other cells and direct browser/device interaction remain to audit for §19.

## Imported file values (2026-11-14)

Material/Cupertino file array controls, scalar picker controls and compact table cells now show imported wrong-type JSON through an English/Bulgarian invalid-value marker. Picker actions and compact inputs expose the value in error semantics. An invalid array can be explicitly cleared to `[]` when the schema and action policy allow it; scalar Clear removes an invalid stored value. Eight paired mounted JVM cases cover object and null imports, missing data, host/locale replacement, zero mount writes and explicit repair. Full extended-renderers JVM passes 425 tests and examples-compose JVM passes 1,724, both with zero failures/skips. JS, Wasm and iOS simulator test sources compile. Direct picker/device behavior and other cells remain open for §19.

## Out-of-domain null dropdown values (2026-11-14)

Material/Cupertino plain-enum and const-oneOf dropdowns now distinguish a missing value from an explicit `null` outside the choices. Standard and searchable selectors display `null` and an English/Bulgarian outside-choices status in visible and accessible feedback, without writing a substitute. Eight mounted JVM cases cover host/locale replacement and one explicit valid selection; six existing selector-description cases now check that the unknown value stays in the accessible state. The full examples-compose JVM suite passes 1,732 tests, Material 77 and Cupertino six, all with zero failures/skips. JS, Wasm and iOS simulator test sources compile. Other choice presentations and browser/device interaction remain open for §19.

## Imported null in array choices (2026-11-14)

Material/Cupertino multi-select and chips controls now distinguish missing data from an explicit non-array `null` by using the mapped data-presence flag. They show `Current value: null` with English/Bulgarian text and associate it with the Add action's error semantics. Full examples-compose JVM passes 1,736 tests, Material 77 and Cupertino six, all with zero failures/skips; JS, Wasm and iOS simulator test sources compile. Material/Cupertino sliders already displayed the exact imported invalid JSON beside a provisional thumb. The shared Material/Cupertino enum-array renderer already displayed imported non-array JSON, including explicit `null`. ## Conflicting table-cell format fallback (2026-11-15)

Two mounted Material/Cupertino cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and isolated Wasm browser for a date-schema table column with authored password cell format. Neither compact temporal cell claims the conflict; the ordinary date Control presents its picker at the original row path. An invalid ISO draft leaves data unchanged with zero writes, and a valid `MM/DD/YYYY` draft commits exactly one ISO date write. The normative-candidate, spec-coverage, registry-drift and whitespace checks pass. ## Default array-shape mutation and validity (2026-11-15)

Two new mounted Material/Cupertino cases cycle through default primitive-string, flat-object and nested-object arrays with `minItems: 1` and no authored table/detail presentation. An Add creates `""` for the primitive row and `{}` for either object row with no declared property defaults. A Delete returns the array to empty. Each action writes once, and combined validity moves Invalid → Valid → Invalid. The focused JVM, iOS simulator and JS browser reports initially passed eight cases each; the expanded ten-case suite below also covers this behavior on Wasm browser. Normative-candidate, spec-coverage and whitespace checks pass. Composed-schema selection remains open.

## Default array-shape row repair (2026-11-15)

Two more mounted Material/Cupertino cases start with a `minLength` error in a primitive row, a flat object property and a nested object property, using the default array presentation each time. Each invalid row has a visible accessible error indicator and Invalid combined status. Replacing its sole text field commits to the original row path exactly once, changes validity to Valid and removes the indicator. Fresh JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser `DefaultArrayShapeUiTest` reports each pass ten cases with zero failures or skips. The first Wasm class-only filter selected one skipped aggregate; `--tests '*DefaultArrayShapeUiTest*'` executed the ten cases. Normative-candidate, spec-coverage and whitespace checks pass. Composed-schema selection remains open.

## Scoped array property inside allOf (2026-11-15)

A combined Material/Cupertino registry test selects the default table for a flat object array and expandable details for a nested object array when the `rows` property is declared inside an `allOf` branch; an authored `table: true` overrides the nested default. The eight-case `ArraySelectionTest` suite passes on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. Two mounted cases edit an invalid flat or nested field at `rows[0]` once and restore whole-schema validity; the twelve-case `DefaultArrayShapeUiTest` suite passes on all four targets with zero failures or skips. A root `allOf` Control still uses the dedicated combinator renderer; these cases exercise a Control scoped to its array property. Other composition contexts remain open.

## Distinct alternative and conditional array scopes (2026-11-15)

A combined Material/Cupertino registry matrix selects flat array tables and nested expandable details for distinct scoped properties in `anyOf`, `oneOf`, `then` and `else` branches, in both registry orders; explicit `table: true` overrides nested defaults. The focused nine-case `ArraySelectionTest` passes on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. Two mounted cases cycle through six active branch/shape combinations, repairing each `minLength` error with one write to the exact array row path and restoring whole-schema validity; the fourteen-case `DefaultArrayShapeUiTest` suite passes on all four targets with zero failures or skips. Overlapping incompatible branches still need a data-aware editing-schema contract.

## Type-selected overlapping editing branches (2026-11-15)

Shared dispatch now selects a top-level `oneOf` or `anyOf` editing branch only when the stored field's JSON type matches exactly one candidate schema. It keeps the original root schema for whole-form validation. Missing values, explicit null without a matching type, overlapping integer/number branches, a shared root property or required contract, and extra `allOf`/conditional constraints retain the existing deterministic lookup. Integer classification uses JSON syntax, including values beyond `Long`. Fresh three-case pure reports pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, including the shared root required guard; the eighteen-case mounted JVM suite also passes after that guard. Four mounted Material/Cupertino cases start with a string, commit a string edit, accept an integer host replacement without an implicit write, then commit a JSON integer through the newly selected editor. Earlier fresh eighteen-case `ComposedScopeSelectionUiTest` reports pass on all four targets. Predicate-based and nested ambiguity resolution remain open.

## Equal ListWithDetail row selection (2026-11-15)

The full iOS simulator and JS browser examples suites each exposed a previously uncovered equal-row move failure in `ListDetailIdentityUiTest`: the move published data but the selected detail remained bound to the old path when the host's new JSON was structurally equal. Material and Cupertino ListWithDetail now update their selected occurrence immediately after dispatch, while their existing effect reconciles later host changes. Fresh JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser identity reports each pass nine cases, including path-specific detail UI schemas for two equal rows. The full examples-compose suites pass 1,842 JVM, 1,814 iOS simulator and 1,813 JS browser cases with zero failures/skips; the focused Wasm examples report passes 27 cases. Normative-candidate, spec-coverage, registry and whitespace checks pass.

## Nested property scopes in overlapping branches (2026-11-15)

The bounded `oneOf`/`anyOf` editing-schema chooser now maps Control scopes made solely of JSON Schema `properties` steps to the corresponding nested data pointer. It preserves JSON Pointer escapes and stays within root dispatch; array-item contexts still use existing selection. Material and Cupertino mounted cases start at `record.value` with a string, commit a string edit, accept an integer host replacement without a write, then commit an integer at the same nested path. Branch predicates, array-item contexts, ambiguous types and direct device interaction remain open.

## Array-detail property scopes in overlapping branches (2026-11-15)

Dispatch now resolves the stored Control value from its current data context before selecting a uniquely type-matched `oneOf` or `anyOf` editing branch. An expanded array detail supplies the item schema and its row path, so a Control scoped to `#/properties/value` can switch editors after the host replaces `rows[0].value`. ## ListWithDetail item branch selection (2026-11-15)

The selected ListWithDetail row also supplies its item schema and row path to shared dispatch. Four mounted Material/Cupertino cases for `oneOf` and `anyOf` show an initial string editor, accept an integer host replacement without a write, reselect the row under ListWithDetail's identity policy, and commit an integer at the original row path. Fresh thirty-case `ComposedScopeSelectionUiTest` reports pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures/skips. ## Shared flat alternative table columns (2026-11-15)

Object-array items whose `oneOf` or `anyOf` branches declare identical flat scalar fields now form ordinary table columns. The default shape tester selects the table for this case; nested or differently named branch fields still select expandable details. Each table row resolves a uniquely type-matched property schema from its own stored value before cell tester selection, preview, and editing, while whole-form validation retains the authored schema. Four new mounted Material/Cupertino cases cover automatic and explicit tables with a string row beside an integer row, typed edits in both rows, and string-to-integer host replacement without an implicit write. Fresh ten-case `ArraySelectionTest` and thirty-four-case `ComposedScopeSelectionUiTest` reports pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures/skips. Full JVM suites pass 458 extended-renderer and 1,858 examples-compose cases with zero failures/skips. Normative-candidate, spec-coverage, registry and whitespace checks pass. Branch predicates, differing alternative columns, ambiguous types and direct device interaction remain open.

## Required const editing discriminators (2026-11-15)

When several `oneOf` or `anyOf` editing candidates share the stored JSON type at a direct property scope, dispatch now examines required sibling `const` fields in the current object. It chooses a branch only if every type-matching candidate has such a discriminator and exactly one matches. Missing or duplicate discriminator matches retain first-match lookup; document validation still uses the authored schema. Four new mounted Material/Cupertino plain-versus-password cases switch the editor after host replacement without a data write. Four more mounted table cases verify that each row independently presents the password affordance as its sibling mode changes, then edits the original row path. Fresh five-case pure and forty-two-case mounted reports pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures/skips. Full JVM suites pass 537 core cases with zero failures and 13 existing skips, plus 1,866 examples-compose cases with zero failures/skips. Normative-candidate, spec-coverage, registry and whitespace checks pass. Other predicates, nested discriminators, differing alternative columns and direct device interaction remain open.

## Nested required const editing discriminators (2026-11-15)

The required sibling `const` chooser now resolves the edited field's parent object in both the candidate branch schema and current data. It supports property-only nested Control scopes and JSON Pointer escaped parent names while retaining the original root-property precedence guard. Four new mounted Material/Cupertino `oneOf`/`anyOf` cases switch a nested plain string editor to password after host replacement, make no implicit write, then commit at the nested path. Fresh six-case pure and forty-six-case mounted reports pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures/skips. Full JVM suites pass 538 core cases with zero failures and 13 existing skips, plus 1,870 examples-compose cases with zero failures/skips. Normative-candidate, spec-coverage, registry and whitespace checks pass. Other predicates, differing alternative columns and direct device interaction remain open.

## Direct rendering for one composition branch (2026-11-15)

Material/Cupertino object `oneOf` and `anyOf` controls now show a sole branch without a dropdown or tab. A missing oneOf value also exposes its editor without creating data. The single-branch header retains the control label, branch label and help text; enclosing properties and existing error dispatch remain in place. Six mounted cases cover both families and keywords, absence of navigation, zero mount writes and one explicit edit. ## Local feedback for displayed object branches (2026-11-15)

Material/Cupertino `oneOf` and `anyOf` object renderers now compile and validate the displayed branch into a child-only state. A branch error on a nested field reaches its ordinary visible and accessible error path, while the document remains valid and `onChange` reports zero document errors. Feedback defaults on; a Control `validateActiveBranch` boolean overrides global config, including explicit false. Disabled feedback and hidden/disabled validation modes skip this extra validation. A validator/schema-keyed Compose cache retains the compiled feedback validator. Root-level branch errors, scalar combinator editors, mixed-type delegation, unresolved references, and direct device interaction remain to cover before the complete spec rule is verified.

## Object-level active-branch errors (2026-11-15)

The renderer-local feedback state now also feeds the combinator control's own error presentation, preserving normal translation and pre-touch filtering. A displayed branch with `minProperties: 2` shows its object-level error while the original anyOf/oneOf document remains valid through a different branch. Control `validateActiveBranch: false` suppresses it; an anyOf host replacement that satisfies the branch removes it without an onChange write. Full examples-compose JVM passes 1,896 tests with zero failures. Scalar combinator editors, mixed-type delegation, unresolved references and direct device interaction remain open.

## Fully valid combinator branch preference (2026-11-15)

The pinned mixed-control Branch feedback fixture exposed an initial selection error: structural matching chose Short text even though its `maxLength` failed and Long text validated. `findFittingSchemaIndex` now selects a fully valid branch first, then uses structural matching when none validates so an invalid value still has an editable branch. A new core test covers both paths. The mounted fixture compares the two editors from the imported schema and data: choosing Short text yields accessible local feedback only where `validateActiveBranch` is enabled, switching back clears it, no data write occurs, and the document remains valid. Full JVM core/examples suites pass 539/1,898 cases with zero failures. iOS simulator and JS browser reports for the new fixture and adjusted twenty-case feedback class have zero failures. The full combined non-JVM run failed at the existing Wasm Karma reporter `specSuccess` exception; a subsequent focused Wasm run for the changed classes passed. Normative-candidate, spec-coverage, renderer-registry and whitespace checks pass. Scalar editor feedback, mixed-type delegation and direct device interaction remain open.

## Imported discriminated table row repair (2026-11-15)

Four mounted Material/Cupertino cases cover automatic `oneOf` and `anyOf` tables with an imported row whose required `mode` discriminator is missing or unknown. The synthesized enum cell exposes branch choices; choosing `secret` preserves the row's existing `secret` field, writes once, clears document errors, and replaces the fallback cell with its password editor. ## Shared-column discriminated Add (2026-11-15)

The branch chooser now also applies when flat alternatives have identical columns and unique required `const` discriminator values. Add presents the branch menu and writes a row with the selected discriminator instead of an ambiguous default object. A pure core case covers `oneOf` and `anyOf`; four mounted Material/Cupertino cases confirm one write and a valid document. ## Numeric const equality in branch selection (2026-11-15)

Required `const` discriminators now compare numeric JSON by exact decimal value for both automatic table branch choices and active property editors. Equivalent spellings such as `1`, `1.0` and `1e0`, and signed zero, cannot identify different branches. A stored `1.0` or `2e0` selects the branch authored with `1` or `2`; values whose exponent exceeds the exact normalizer's range remain ambiguous. The normalizer no longer treats an overflowing exponent as zero. Pure core tests cover equivalent and distinct numeric/string values, overflow ambiguity and both combinator keywords. Four mounted Material/Cupertino cases show a host replacement switching to the password editor without a write, then one valid edit. Focused core JVM/iOS and mounted JVM/iOS cases pass. Full JVM suites pass 549 core tests (13 existing skips), 81 Material tests and 1,934 examples-compose tests with zero failures. Two initial JS retries stalled during webpack preparation; the later 80-case browser reports below include these cases.

## Numeric choice presentation equality (2026-11-15)

Material/Cupertino enum, const-oneOf and radio controls now recognize numerically equivalent stored values without normalizing or writing them. The oneOf checkbox-array renderers use the same comparison for checked state, unknown-value classification, duplicate prevention and removal at the original occurrence. Ten mounted cases cover both families, dropdown/radio presentations and checkbox arrays, including host replacement from `1.0` to `2e0`; all pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Focused core discriminator tests also pass on JS and Wasm browsers (14 tests on each), and both renderer modules compile for JS and Wasm. Full JVM suites pass 549 core tests (13 existing skips), 81 Material, six Cupertino and 1,944 examples-compose tests with zero failures. The later focused browser reports below pass all ten mounted cases on JS and Wasm.

The smaller core JS and Wasm browser suites each pass 14 discriminator and numeric-value cases.
