## Recursive numeric equality in structural choices (2026-11-15)

The shared JSON Schema value comparison now descends into objects and arrays. This lets an option authored as `{"x":1}` match imported `{"x":1.0}`, and `[1,2]` match `[1.0,2e0]`, without normalizing the stored value or writing on mount. A false nested value stays distinct; an exponent outside the exact decimal range stays uncertain. Required sibling object `const` values use the same comparison for active editor selection. Core `AlternativeObjectPropertiesTest` and `ActiveEditingSchemaTest` pass five and ten cases, respectively, on JS and Wasm browsers, plus focused JVM and iOS runs. Six new mounted Material/Cupertino structural-choice cases bring `NumericChoiceEqualityUiTest` to 16 passing cases on each of JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser, with zero failures or skips in fresh reports. Full JVM suites pass 550 core tests (13 existing skips), 81 Material, six Cupertino and 1,950 examples-compose tests with zero failures. An initial JS run stalled during Karma preparation with low free disk; after clearing only generated output, the focused retry passed. Full browser suites and physical-device interaction remain open.

## Duration and cron validation channels (2026-11-15)

Four mounted Material/Cupertino cases begin with an invalid committed `duration` or `cron` value and one application-supplied additional error. Combined validity reports one schema error and one additional error; a valid text edit clears only the schema error, and replacing the host error collection then restores Valid without another data write. No format failure is republished as an additional error. `FormatErrorChannelUiTest` passes four cases with zero failures or skips on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. This provides bounded evidence for §15.6-01; other registered formats and renderer-owned publication remain to audit.

## Finite generation for unresolved references (2026-11-15)

A mounted single-branch schema with a missing property `$ref` reproduced a stack overflow in UI schema generation before local branch validation ran. The generator now returns a Control when a reference cannot be resolved or repeats at the same schema location; pure tests cover both a missing property reference and a cyclic root reference. Renderer-local feedback also drops the validator's schema-compilation failure instead of presenting it as a field error. Two Material/Cupertino mounted cases show no crash, no local compilation message and no mount write. Full JVM suites pass 541 core and 1,900 examples-compose cases with zero failures. iOS simulator reports show 22 changed generator cases and 22 changed feedback cases with zero failures; unfiltered JS reports include both changed classes with zero failures. Method-filtered Wasm reports show 22 generator cases (three existing skips) and 22 feedback cases with zero failures. The initial combined non-JVM run hit a full disk while writing Gradle results; generated Wasm/JS compiler caches were removed within this workspace, and subsequent runs completed. The unfiltered Wasm Karma reporter remains unreliable.

## Literal global config namespace (2026-11-15)

Serialized JSON cases verify that `layoutDefaults` and `confirmation` read the literal `jsonformsExtended` key and ignore top-level or differently cased aliases. A row-summary gate test verifies the same boundary for `dynamicValues`; mounted Material/Cupertino Button and Markdown cases verify it for `security` and `markup`. The focused JVM reports pass three table-summary and twenty markup UI cases, with zero failures or skips; the matching iOS simulator reports also pass 3/20. The serialized confirmation and layout JVM cases and mounted Button JVM/iOS cases passed in the same turn. These results cover five of the six named global blocks; the experimental `dynamic` block and other consumers still require an audit. Only English and Bulgarian locale content is shipped.

## Authored restrict default through core config (2026-11-15)

The core config reducer previously inserted `restrict: false` for every `SetConfigAction`, including an empty host config. That made the preferred spec default of true indistinguishable from an authored false for consumers of the reduced config. The reducer now leaves `restrict` absent unless the host supplies it. Focused reducer/resolver tests cover omitted, misplaced namespaced and explicit top-level false settings on JVM and iOS simulator (five iOS cases, zero failures); full core JVM (554 tests, 13 existing skips) and examples-compose JVM (1,967 tests) suites pass with zero failures. The Compose entry point already passed authored config directly. Other top-level option readers and browser targets remain to verify.

Four mounted Material/Cupertino cases pass host `showUnfocusedDescription` and `hideRequiredAsterisk` at the top level while supplying the opposite values under `jsonformsExtended`. Each case changes the top-level value live and verifies the unfocused help or required marker changes without a form write. The first iOS link failed under low disk space; clearing only ignored generated test binaries made the retry pass. Other presentation readers and browser interaction remain to audit.

A serialized Control with flat `options.toggle: true` selects the Material and Cupertino switch testers. An authored document now round-trips through the demo editor with its layout, Group, Control, Label, Spacer, Separator, Link, ImageView, ListWithDetail, Categorization, Category, Slot and TemplateLayout fields intact. The case includes names, a rule, flat options and an unknown template payload; parsing, serializing and editor text produce the original JSON object. ## Family capability declaration (2026-11-15)

`docs/renderer-capabilities.md` now states the registered Material, Cupertino and extended profiles, the shared OptimumCode validator dialect/extensions, target evidence boundaries, and the explicit AG Grid, Monaco and TemplateLayout fallback behavior. It links the exact registry inventory and browser lifecycle gate; registration is not treated as blanket conformance. ## Live layout default precedence (2026-11-15)

Four mounted Material/Cupertino cases exercise live `config.jsonformsExtended.layoutDefaults` changes. Changing `gridColumns` from two to four narrows only a row that inherits it; another row with local `gridColumns: 2` retains its span width. A misplaced top-level `layoutDefaults` value does not affect either row. Changing global `wrap` from false to true moves the second fixed child to a new line only in the inheriting row; a local `wrap: false` keeps its row unwrapped. Both pairs pass focused JVM and iOS simulator runs with zero failures or data writes. Browser/Android geometry and more nested layouts remain open.

## Function-rule renderer context (2026-11-15)

Layout, splitter, direct presentation, control and cell visibility now evaluate function rules with the renderer's parent path and host config. Enable and read-only mapper rules receive the same context; category filtering passes the category container's path as well. A mounted two-field row exposed the defect: the parent filter received the path/config, but the child control mapper received null values and hid the field. New core mapper tests cover SHOW, ENABLE and READONLY with both matching and nonmatching config, and category filtering checks both path and config. Material/Cupertino mounted rows and splitters react to a live config change without a data write; the splitter loses its divider when only one pane remains. The full core (557 tests, 13 existing skips), extended-renderers (483 tests) and examples-compose (1,981 tests) JVM suites passed with zero failures; focused core iOS simulator tests (68 cases), both mounted layout cases and both splitter cases also passed. The first combined mounted iOS link exhausted disk space; deleting only ignored generated test binaries and running the modules separately produced passing results. Browser/Android interaction and structural placeholders remain open. Only English and Bulgarian locale content is shipped.

## Empty structural layout participation (2026-11-15)

An empty nested VerticalLayout reproduced an extra row column in both renderer families. A shared effective-child resolver now removes HorizontalLayout and VerticalLayout nodes with no visible descendants before row, column, wrapped-row and splitter sizing. It keeps visible Groups, which can have headings even with no children, and authored Spacers. Core tests cover nested empty and rule-hidden descendants with path/config context. Paired mounted Material/Cupertino tests verify equal row widths, unchanged vertical gap after live insertion of an empty layout, and a two-pane splitter with one divider instead of two. Full JVM suites pass 558 core tests (13 existing skips), 485 extended-renderer tests and 1,985 examples-compose tests, with zero failures. The focused shared test, four mounted row/column tests and two splitter tests pass on iOS simulator. Framework comments, fragments and placeholders are not authored Kotlin UI-schema element types; custom no-content renderer dispatch and browser/Android geometry remain unverified. Only English and Bulgarian locale content is shipped.

## Parent minimum width mounted contract (2026-11-15)

Two new Material/Cupertino mounted cases exercise §7.2 `minItemWidth` through the real registry. With a 260dp row, 10dp gap and 140dp parent minimum, the second control wraps. Giving that control `options.layout.minWidth: 60` returns it to the first row on live UI-schema replacement without a data write. Both cases pass on JVM. The same common test source compiles for JS and iOS simulator, but runtime evidence is still open: the initial JS command stopped in unrelated webcomponent production webpack, and the retry with `-Pjsonforms.skipWebcomponentResources=true` exhausted disk during Kotlin/JS test-executable compilation. The focused iOS test compiled, but repeated native links exhausted disk in clang/ld and `dsymutil` before execution. Only ignored partial generated outputs were removed. No browser or iOS pass is claimed for these two cases.

## Diagnostics on empty structural layouts (2026-11-15)

Filtering empty nested layouts from parent sizing had also suppressed their authored container-option diagnostics. `ReportLayoutDiagnostics` now reports invalid options on visible structural layouts omitted from sizing and records their index path; the Material layout renderer also checks options on an empty root before returning an empty layout. The shared diagnostic builder includes the offending key on invalid and unsupported option reports. Paired mounted Material/Cupertino tests cover a filtered empty VerticalLayout with unsupported `gridColumns` and an empty root HorizontalLayout with invalid `gridColumns`; the full diagnostics class passes after adding the structural-path assertion. Full JVM suites passed before that final assertion: 558 core tests (13 existing skips), 83 Material, six Cupertino and 1,991 examples-compose tests, with zero failures. The pinned §7.2 ledger text was also corrected from an intrinsic-width claim to the spec's resolved-fit/minimum-constraints wording. Browser, iOS runtime and Android interaction for these diagnostic cases remain unverified. Only English and Bulgarian locale content is shipped.

The common test source and shared diagnostics implementation compile for iOS simulator, JS and Wasm (`:examples-compose:compileTestKotlinIosSimulatorArm64`, `compileTestKotlinJs` and `compileTestKotlinWasmJs`). A first combined JS/Wasm command reached Wasm test compilation, then failed in the unrelated webcomponent production webpack task before the overall command could complete; a retry with `-Pjsonforms.skipWebcomponentResources=true` passed. The webpack error exposed a wrong relative path for the browser `os` shim: generated webpack config runs from `build/js/packages/jsonforms-kotlin-webcomponent`, four levels below the repository root. The shim path is corrected; `:webcomponent:jsBrowserProductionWebpack --offline` now succeeds with four webpack warnings, and all eight `scripts/test-webcomponent-registration.mjs` cases pass. These results prove cross-target compilation, bundling and registration checks, not browser rendering or complete host parity. Only ignored generated browser outputs were removed after the disk-limited attempt.

## Reserved layout hints and production webcomponent browser run (2026-11-15)

Two mounted Material/Cupertino JVM cases add `options.layout.start` and `options.layout.responsive` during a live form. Both keep field positions and widths unchanged, report no author diagnostic, and publish no data write, matching their reserved-and-ignored contract. The focused class reports two passing cases; non-JVM geometry remains open.

The existing Chrome webcomponent interaction script can now serve a selected production bundle and its JS chunk. With `WEBCOMPONENT_BUNDLE_MODE=production node scripts/test-webcomponent-browser.mjs`, one mounted browser case passed against the corrected production webpack output. It verifies action event details and form context, host `waitUntil` completion/rejection, script opt-in and CSP rejection, locale fallback and live Bulgarian locale, and RTL placement. `:webcomponent:jsBrowserProductionWebpack --offline` and all eight Node registration cases also pass. This is real production-bundle browser interaction evidence for part of §30.1 host parity; native/webcomponent equivalence, ShadowRoot overlays and full browser renderer suites remain open. Only English and Bulgarian locale content is shipped.

## Group persistence and supported locales (2026-11-15)

Paired mounted Material/Cupertino Group cases now edit a constrained field into an invalid value, collapse and reopen the Group, and verify that the stored value, schema error, combined Invalid status and write count persist. Two further cases close a Group, replace host data and change an unrelated namespaced config option, then change the effective `collapsed` boolean; local expansion persists until that boolean changes, with no header write. The focused Group class passes on JVM. A demo regression locks the selectable locale enum to English and Bulgarian; its focused JVM class passes. German tags remain test-only unsupported inputs for English fallback and are not a shipped locale. Browser/native Group interaction remains open.

## Group validation indicator locale defaults (2026-11-15)

The Material and Cupertino Group validation indicators now use shared English/Bulgarian defaults for no count, one error and multiple errors. The host translator still receives the same `group.validationIndicator` key and can override the selected default. A core test covers all three Bulgarian forms, English fallback and host precedence. Paired mounted tests switch from English to regional Bulgarian and then hide the count, checking the accessible label and zero data writes. The focused core and Group validation JVM classes pass. Other containers' plural catalogs and non-JVM accessibility remain open.

## Category indicator locale defaults (2026-11-15)

Category tab, step and accordion headers now resolve the built-in Group data-presence label and a new Category validation label from the active English/Bulgarian locale, even when no host translator is installed. The host still receives the stable `category.validationIndicator` key and may override the selected fallback. A core test covers no-count, singular and plural Bulgarian forms plus override precedence. Six mounted Material/Cupertino cases change locale to `bg-BG`, then disable the count; both accessible labels update without a data write. The focused core and Category JVM test classes pass. Array and other container indicators still need the same built-in locale audit; browser/native accessibility remains open.

## Array summary and item indicator locale defaults (2026-11-15)

Shared array validation text now supplies English/Bulgarian defaults for summary and one-based item labels, with no-count, singular and plural forms. A core test covers all forms and override arguments. Four mounted JVM cases change from English to regional Bulgarian and hide the count on an array control and list-with-detail in both families, checking summary and item accessible labels with zero data writes. Existing array validation summary tests with host overrides also pass. The focused core and array summary JVM classes pass. Table, tuple, composite and other array status labels still need built-in locale review; non-JVM accessibility remains open.

## Tuple position indicator locale defaults (2026-11-15)

Complex tuple positions now use English/Bulgarian built-in validation indicator text for no count, singular and plural counts. Paired mounted Material/Cupertino cases switch live to `bg-BG`, hide the count and verify the accessible label without a data write. The full tuple UI JVM class passes, including its existing host translator test. Table/composite labels and browser/native accessibility remain open.

## Table cell and row-detail indicator locale defaults (2026-11-15)

Material table composite cells and row-detail markers now resolve built-in English/Bulgarian accessible labels. Host translators keep the stable `composite.validationIndicator` and `rowDetail.validationIndicator` keys with label/index/count parameters. Core tests cover the forms and override precedence. Other table variants, pagination labels and browser/native accessibility remain open.

## Collection pager locale defaults (2026-11-15)

Material and Cupertino collection pagers now use English/Bulgarian built-in navigation, page-size, page-count, first-error and error-page labels while preserving the existing host translator keys. The English error-page label now says `1 error` for a single error. Core tests cover Bulgarian labels, singular/plural counts and host override arguments. Mounted Material/Cupertino table and list-with-detail tests switch live to `bg-BG`, follow an error-page link and make no data write. The full JVM table and list pagination classes pass. Tuple-tail, additional-properties, row-detail navigation and kitchen-sink fixture JVM classes also pass after updating singular-error assertions. Narrow/RTL placement, keyboard interaction and browser/native accessibility remain open.

The visible page changes forward and back without a data write. The focused cases pass. Browser/native keyboard behavior, narrow and RTL geometry and optional top-pager state sharing remain open.

## Paged edit identity on host replacement (2026-11-15)

Paired mounted Material/Cupertino JVM tests now open an additional-property rename draft on page two, then replace host data. The stale Save action disappears, page navigation re-enables, the host value is shown and no data write occurs. A second paired test selects a list-with-detail item on page two, replaces the array with a different item at that position, and verifies that selection clears rather than redirecting the detail editor. The focused cases and full additional-properties pagination JVM class pass. Sort/filter identity, deletion/reorder interaction and non-JVM pending drafts remain open.

Paired mounted list-with-detail JVM cases now move a selected item from page two across the page boundary, then edit its detail; the edit updates the moved item's new index. Another pair confirms deletion of an earlier item on the current page, then edits the shifted selected item at its new index. Each sequence publishes exactly the intended move/delete and edit writes. The full list-detail pagination JVM class passes. Sort/filter identity and non-JVM pending-draft interaction remain open.

The changed common UI test sources compile for JS and iOS simulator. A focused `:examples-compose:jsBrowserTest --tests '*ListDetailPaginationUiTest*'` reached `compileTestDevelopmentExecutableKotlinJs`, then exhausted disk while writing Kotlin's incremental executable cache; no browser test started. The incomplete ignored cache was removed. `:examples-compose:compileTestKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` passes, without native linking or runtime execution. These are compile-only observations and do not close non-JVM pagination interaction.

## Strict pagination option shape (2026-11-15)

The shared pagination resolver now accepts Boolean or object values only. A quoted `"false"`, numeric primitive or invalid scoped primitive is ignored so the next valid scoped or presentation default applies; explicit Boolean false still disables pagination. Core tests cover those transitions and whole-value precedence. Paired mounted Material/Cupertino-host table cases change local and scoped values live and verify navigation visibility with no data write. The focused core class and full table, additional-properties, tuple-tail and list-with-detail pagination JVM classes pass. The atomic option-shape requirement is recorded as partial pending other-target runtime and malformed nested-object cases.

An authored empty `pageSizeOptions` array, or one containing no positive integers, now falls back to `[5, 10, 25, 50]` before including the initial page size. This keeps the selector available when it can change the visible result; a valid authored singleton still hides the unnecessary selector. Core tests cover both invalid forms, and paired Material/Cupertino-host mounted tests replace valid choices with invalid ones live without a data write. The focused core and full table pagination JVM classes pass. Other malformed object fields and non-JVM runtime remain open.

## Web component system color scheme (2026-11-15)

The web component now resolves explicit `light` and `dark` themes directly and resolves `system` or an absent theme through `prefers-color-scheme`. Node registration tests cover theme resolution, live refresh, and listener lifecycle (10/10 pass). `:webcomponent:compileKotlinJs --offline` passes. A browser assertion for explicit and system transitions was added to the existing mounted test, but was not run: no generated web component bundle is available and the disk has about 289 MiB free. ShadowRoot styling and native/demo theme behavior remain separate open requirements.

Paired Material/Cupertino-style mounted JVM demo tests now edit the Data JSON draft through the real workspace. An additional paired mounted JVM sequence reproduced Data draft loss: editing the live form after leaving an unapplied Data JSON draft replaced the draft text. The full `DemoEditorLifecycleUiTest` and `EditorDraftTest` classes pass after the fix. Changed common main and test sources compile for iOS simulator; simulator runtime and simultaneous Demo/Data interaction remain open.

Paired Material/Cupertino-style mounted JVM tests now keep the form and Data editor visible together in the Demo and Data layout. Editing the form retains an unapplied JSON draft; applying that draft updates the form; another form edit refreshes the now-clean editor. The full editor lifecycle class passes. `DemoSplitPaneUiTest` also passes on JVM, covering an initial 75/25 split in a forced wide container, minimum widths, narrow stacking and RTL progress/keyboard/touch resize. The default test viewport clamps a requested 1200 dp width to 1000 px, so the wide geometry assertion uses `requiredWidth`. Browser/native interaction remains open.

## JVM suite gate and read-only presentation (2026-11-15)

The full `:extended-renderers:jvmTest :examples-compose:jvmTest --offline` run passes 511 and 2,086 tests respectively, with zero failures, errors or skips in the XML reports. This covers the recent editor error/keyboard and full-registry changes through the broader JVM suites. A later test-only follow-up adds paired Material/Cupertino checks for `separateReadonlyFromDisabled`: text remains enabled and read-only, while Boolean widgets remain disabled because checkbox/switch APIs have no text-style read-only mode. The focused `ReadonlyRulePrecedenceUiTest` JVM class passes and its iOS simulator test source compiles. §S29-01 remains partial pending other control/cell families and runtime interaction on browser/native hosts.

## Canonical variant applicability (2026-11-15)

The full Material/Cupertino extended renderer registries now have explicit selection assertions for the four canonical §5.4 variants. `chips` selects for free string arrays and `multi-select` for finite choices; unsupported `multi-select` on a free string array and `chips` on an integer array fall back to the ordinary array renderer. `stepper` and `accordion` select their family renderers for valid Categorization elements, while an unrelated `chips` Categorization variant falls back to family tabs. The focused `ArraySelectionTest` JVM class passes and its changed common test source compiles for iOS simulator. Existing mounted classes separately cover the four presentations; mounted fallback interaction and browser/native runtime remain open, so §S5.4-01 is partial.

Paired mounted `CanonicalVariantFallbackUiTest` cases now verify the non-finite `multi-select` request exposes ordinary array Add, preserves the original item and adds one item with one write. A Categorization carrying unrelated `variant: chips` renders tabs, switches to the second field and makes no data write. The focused four-case JVM class passes and its common test source compiles for iOS simulator. Integer-array chips fallback and browser/native runtime remain open.

Two further mounted Material/Cupertino cases verify `variant: chips` on an integer array uses ordinary array Add, retains the original numeric value and adds one item with one write. The expanded six-case `CanonicalVariantFallbackUiTest` JVM class passes and compiles for iOS simulator. Browser/native runtime remains open for §S5.4-01.

The full-registry `ArraySelectionTest` now also asserts that a `$dynamic`-shaped object in `options.variant` does not select a static array presentation in either family; the automatic checkbox renderer still wins. Existing cases show top-level config variant cannot select that presentation either. The focused JVM class passes and iOS simulator test sources compile. The experimental overlay profile is separate from this static-profile behavior, so §S5.4-02 remains partial pending broader authoring/runtime evidence.

The pinned extended authoring schema now has explicit probes in `check-imported-authoring.mjs`: literal `chips`/`multi-select` Control and `stepper`/`accordion` Categorization variants pass, while `$dynamic` objects in either variant position fail static validation. The complete authoring gate passes seven registered schemas, 49 source entries, 46 imports and 194 local documents/registries. Broader option and target-runtime checks remain open.

## Additional-error integration boundary (2026-11-15)

`JsonForms` always wraps renderer dispatch in `JsonFormsStateProvider`. That provider creates `JsonFormsComposeController`, whose owned-error store is present by construction, and supplies it through a required `LocalJsonFormsContext`; `useJsonForms()` throws if a renderer is composed outside the provider. File publication uses the controller even when the host omits `additionalErrors`. Focused `JsonFormsComposeControllerTest` (13 cases) and Material/Cupertino `UnrestrictedSizeOwnsValidity` mounted tests (two cases) pass on JVM with zero failures/skips. Thus the spec's conditional missing-integration diagnostic has no reachable state in the supported Kotlin host. Direct composition outside the provider is not a supported renderer host; this classification must be revisited if one is introduced.

## Browser-only profile boundary recheck (2026-11-15)

The current Web Component still mounts one `ComposeViewport` into a div and has no per-renderer DOM child host or viewport disposal handle. `node --test scripts/test-webcomponent-registration.mjs` passes 10 registration, reconnection, theme and action cases. Focused mounted JVM suites pass five `AgGridFallbackUiTest` cases and 12 `CodeProfileFallbackTest`/`UnsupportedTemplateLayoutTest` cases, zero failures, errors or skips in XML. This confirms the present portable/unsupported fallbacks and leaves AG Grid, Monaco and JSX/Ractive TemplateLayout unimplemented as browser engines. A DOM island with lifecycle disposal and browser interaction tests remains the gate before any web-only adapter claim.

## Web Component bridge registry notice (2026-11-15)

The demo now builds `WebComponentInput` from the current form state and settings through a testable helper. Named UI schemas remain serialized, while registered tester functions cannot cross the iframe attribute boundary; the JS overlay displays an English/Bulgarian notice. The bridge now carries missing-data presence, validation mode and demo config overrides as well as current data, readonly, direction, locale/form-locale override, theme and catalogs. The Web Component observes `data-provided` and `validation-mode`; `jsonforms-change` includes `dataProvided` even when only presence changes, and the demo returns it to Compose. Five JVM bridge tests and 10 Node registration tests pass. JVM, JS, Wasm, Web Component JS and iOS test sources compile. `:examples-compose-android:compileDebugKotlin --offline` stopped at dependency resolution because AndroidX artifacts including `appcompat-resources:1.7.1` and `exifinterface:1.4.2` are not cached; it is not an Android compile result. Browser assertions for the attributes, notice and Web-Component-to-Compose-and-back data round trip were added to `test-demo-browser-url.mjs`; they remain unrun because the demo/Web Component bundle is absent. §§S30.1-01/02 remain partial.

The bridge now also serializes host additional errors, including paths, owner and translation params, to `additional-errors`. The Web Component parses that attribute into `ErrorObject` values and passes them to `JsonForms`. Both demo hosts call `demoFormActionEffect` for language, theme, report/clear server errors and status actions; a status action now marks newly created form data as provided. The iframe data callback retains host errors for unchanged values, like the native host. Six focused bridge and three action JVM tests pass with zero failures or skips; 10 Node registration tests pass; examples JS/Wasm/iOS test sources and Web Component JS compile. Browser server-action assertions were added but remain unrun without the bundle. Android and browser runtime gates remain open.

The `additional-errors` decoder is now a portable core function used by the Web Component. Three focused core JVM tests pass for full error metadata, omitted/empty lists and atomic rejection of malformed payloads. Core iOS simulator and Wasm sources compile, and the Web Component JS consumer compiles after extraction. This verifies decoding independently of the unexecuted browser bundle.

## Web Component browser gate after cache recovery (2026-11-15)

Stopping Gradle and removing only its generated `caches/9.9.0/transforms` directory raised free workspace space from about 405 MiB to 2.0 GiB. `:webcomponent:jsBrowserDevelopmentWebpack` and `:examples-compose:jsBrowserDevelopmentWebpack -Pjsonforms.skipWebcomponentResources=true` both passed. The first sandboxed `node --test` attempt could not bind the local test server (`EPERM`); the approved local-network run reached Chrome. The host round-trip assertion initially queried a field absent from the selected fixture; the corrected assertion reads the Compose Data editor. All 13 `scripts/test-demo-browser-url.mjs` scenarios now pass in Chrome with zero failures/skips, including edited data surviving Web Component→Compose→Web Component, validation/config attributes, registered tester notice, server-error report/clear, form locale/theme actions, history, settings and links.

The standalone `scripts/test-webcomponent-browser.mjs` scenario also passes in Chrome. Its old DOM-button color query did not apply to a Compose canvas; a rendered button-region comparison with an explicit light baseline now verifies explicit light/dark and emulated system light/dark changes after mount, followed by mounted host action completion/rejection. This is browser runtime evidence for §S30.1-04. Registry tester functions, ShadowRoot portals/workers and cross-host settings round trips remain open; §§S30.1-01/02 stay partial. Offline Android dependency resolution remains unverified.

A focused rerun of the Job Chrome scenario passes after extending it to close the Web Component and return to Compose, observe the Bulgarian form action, reopen the component and confirm the Bulgarian form locale, toggled form theme and unchanged data. This adds an executed settings round trip for §S30.1-02. Direction, validation mode and other settings still need mounted switch coverage.

A new fixed-viewport Chrome scenario selects RTL and Validate And Hide through the visible Settings panel, switches to the Web Component, returns to Compose and reopens it. Both `dir=rtl` and `validation-mode=validate-and-hide` survive; the focused scenario passes. Inspection of Chrome's Settings accessibility tree exposed only the focused Close settings tooltip, despite visible Direction and Validation controls. Replacing the panel's empty click absorber with pointer-only handling did not change the tree, so that experiment was reverted. This is an open browser accessibility issue, distinct from the passing pointer and host-attribute round trip. Remaining settings and missing-data transitions still need mounted switch coverage.

Further Chrome inspection found that the Compose viewport's accessibility DOM contained only the active popup's semantics while Settings was open: Close settings tooltip initially, then LTR/RTL choices while the Direction menu was open. Enabling CDP Accessibility, moving focus and changing the panel click absorber did not expose the underlying Settings controls. Temporary probe code was removed. The standalone Web Component Chrome test now sets an authored `additional-errors` attribute and passes an assertion that its decoded path, message and owner enter the mounted Button action context. The browser accessibility issue remains open.

The standalone mounted Chrome test now switches `validation-mode` between Validate And Show, No Validation and Validate And Hide while a `minLength` violation is present. Its Button action context has the schema error in the first and third modes and an empty errors list in No Validation. It then starts with `data=null`, `data-provided=false`, edits a nested `name` text field to `Grace` and observes `jsonforms-change` with the object and `dataProvided=true`. This exposed a reducer bug: nested writes retained the prior absent-data flag. The shared reducer now sets presence on any write unless the action explicitly supplies it. A common core regression test covers the nested case; root presence tests remain. `:core:jvmTest` passes 574 cases with zero failures/errors and 13 existing skips; `:core:compileKotlinWasmJs` and `:core:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` pass offline, and the rebuilt Web Component bundle passes the Chrome scenario. Only English and Bulgarian locales ship.

The demo Chrome host-switch scenario now also sends a missing-data change (`data=null`, `dataProvided=false`) through the mounted Web Component bridge, returns to Compose and reopens the Web Component with the missing state intact. It then enters `Grace` in the first Required fields text input, observes the bridge publish `legalName` with the absent flag removed, and preserves that provided object through another Compose/Web Component switch. All 14 demo Chrome URL scenarios pass. Chrome's accessibility tree exposed the three textboxes with empty names after the missing-state reopen; this is a distinct browser accessibility gap, so the test locates the first input by fixture order and confirms the edited property.

The empty text-input accessibility names came from the shared Material and Cupertino input modifiers: they supplied `contentDescription` only for compact errors and relied on a visually drawn label elsewhere. Both now attach the control label to the editable semantics, retaining compact error text when present. Two paired mounted JVM assertions in `SimpleControlDescriptionUiTest` pass without a data write; their common test source compiles for iOS simulator. Material/Cupertino iOS simulator and Wasm main sources compile, and the rebuilt Web Component Chrome test locates `Legal name` by its accessible name after the absent-data host switch. The full 14-scenario demo Chrome URL suite passes. This closes the observed text-label defect; other role/state and keyboard accessibility remain partial.

## Web Component ShadowRoot mount (2026-11-15)

The local Compose JS runtime exposes `ComposeViewport(HTMLElement)`, whereas the previous ID overload used `document.getElementById`. The Web Component now creates or reuses an open ShadowRoot, appends its viewport host there, and passes that element directly to Compose. Compose adds its own nested shadow tree for canvas and backing-input styles. The rebuilt development bundle passes a Chrome assertion that the component has no light-DOM children, its shadow tree contains a visible canvas, and the nested style includes `.compose-backing-field`. The production webpack bundle also builds offline and passes the same standalone Chrome interaction test. All 14 demo URL Chrome scenarios pass with the development bundle, and 10 Node registration tests pass. Styled popups, worker paths and viewport disposal/reconnect need separate browser evidence; §S30.1-03 is partial.

The standalone Chrome test now opens a Material enum dropdown from a mounted field. Its `Ada` option is visible and styled, remains under the custom element's shadow ancestry, and clicking it emits `jsonforms-change` with `name: Ada` and `dataProvided=true`. Both development and production bundles pass. A separate reconnect probe then removed and reinserted the same element. The ShadowRoot and canvas remained, but the editable textbox never returned to Chrome's accessibility tree during a five-second poll; repeating without a host data update produced the same result. The reconnect probe was removed from the passing suite after recording the failure. The public `ComposeViewport(HTMLElement)` call returns no disposal handle, so simply creating another viewport on reconnect would leave the old Compose window alive. §S30.1-03 stays partial pending a safe lifecycle mechanism, other portal types and worker checks.

The reconnect diagnosis was refined: after selecting `Ada`, Chrome's accessibility tree still exposed the popup layer and no textbox even before any detachment; pressing Escape did not restore the textbox during a five-second poll. The canvas dimensions and accessibility DOM text remained intact across detachment, so the earlier probe did not isolate a reconnect defect. The browser test now detaches and reinserts the element while a plain text control is active, confirms the same ShadowRoot has one viewport and the textbox is accessible again, then opens/selects the enum popup. This passes for both development and production bundles. Popup dismissal/accessibility semantics remain open; `ComposeViewport(HTMLElement)` still provides no handle for a true unmount. The earlier reconnect-failure interpretation above is superseded by this controlled probe.

The Material enum renderer already sets `expanded=false` on option selection. An experiment wrapping `ExposedDropdownMenu` in an explicit `if (expanded)` passed focused JVM choice tests but did not restore the textbox to Chrome's accessibility tree within five seconds; it was reverted. A click outside the form after selection also failed to restore the textbox in the production bundle. These probes narrow the remaining issue to Compose Web popup/accessibility synchronization rather than the renderer's expanded state. The passing browser suite continues to assert popup placement, styling and data publication without claiming dismissal semantics.

## Direct Web Component UI schema testers (2026-11-15)

The Web Component now exposes a JavaScript `uiSchemaRegistry` property for `{tester, uischema}` entries. The Kotlin JS boundary converts a candidate `JsonSchema` to a plain JS object, calls the tester with `(schema, schemaPath, path)`, accepts a finite numeric rank and falls back to not applicable when a tester returns an invalid result or throws. The property setter refreshes the form, including a same-array reassignment after editing entries; parsed entries are memoized between registry updates. A mounted Chrome test confirms the tester receives an object schema, `#/properties/address` and `address`, selects its labeled nested detail, falls back to generated detail after replacing the tester with rank `-1`, restores the registered detail on rank `10`, and falls back again when the property is cleared. Both development and production bundles pass the final Chrome scenario; all 14 demo URL scenarios and 11 Node registration tests pass. The iframe bridge still uses JSON attributes and cannot transfer functions, so its limitation notice remains correct. Only English and Bulgarian are shipped.

The custom element's `connectedCallback` now upgrades an own `uiSchemaRegistry` property assigned before definition: it removes that own property and reassigns the value through the live setter before the Kotlin mount. A Node regression test verifies the original tester function reaches mount and a later replacement triggers refresh. All 12 registration tests pass; both development and production mounted Chrome scenarios continue to pass with the updated registration script. The bundle code did not change in this follow-up.

The mounted Chrome server now inserts a tester-backed property before loading either registration script or Kotlin bundle. After custom-element upgrade, both development and production tests verify the property is served by the class setter (no own property) and its tester function remains callable. A later tester that throws causes generated object detail to render and emits a bubbling `jsonforms-diagnostic` with code `uischema.testerFailed`, data path `address` and the thrown message; the form remains interactive. Both bundles rebuild and pass the browser scenario, all 14 demo Chrome URL scenarios pass, and 12 Node registration tests remain green. This covers the direct-property registry failure boundary; the iframe bridge still cannot serialize tester functions.

## Strict canonical presentation option types (2026-11-15)

A paired mounted Boolean fixture regression exposed that quoted `options.toggle: "true"` selected a switch. The shared `optionIs` comparison now requires a JSON Boolean for Boolean options and JSON numbers for integer/double options; it no longer coerces quoted strings. The fixture confirms `options.switch: true` and quoted `toggle` use the ordinary checkbox, while authored `toggle: true` selects a switch, without form writes. A core tester regression covers Boolean, integer, double and string distinctions. Focused core and mounted tests pass on JVM and iOS simulator. Full JVM suites pass 576 core tests (13 existing skips), 2,111 examples-compose tests and 518 extended-renderers tests, zero failures/errors. Core JS and Wasm main compilation also pass. §§S5-01/04 remain partial pending JS/Wasm/Android runtime execution and broader alias review.

The same audit found readonly resolution reading `JsonPrimitive.content` directly, which treated a quoted `"true"` as a Boolean. UI `readonly`/`readOnly` options and schema `readOnly` now require actual JSON Boolean values. A mapper regression verifies quoted values are ignored and canonical true is honored; focused JVM/iOS simulator cases pass. Full core JVM passes 577 tests with 13 existing skips, and the full examples-compose JVM suite passes 2,111 tests with zero failures/skips. Core JS and Wasm main compilation pass. §S15.1-01 remains partial pending target runtime and mutation-path verification.

Cross-target core browser follow-up: the first JS browser run exposed that Kotlin/JS routes both `Int` and `Double` values through the numeric tester's `Int` branch, rejecting a decimal expected value. Numeric option matching now uses one `Number` branch with strict unquoted JSON number parsing. Full core suites pass 577 JVM tests and 576 each on JS and Wasm browser, with 13 existing skips per target and zero failures/errors; the focused 13-case iOS simulator `TestersTest` also passes. This establishes the shared option and readonly regressions on browser targets, while mounted Compose browser interaction remains open.

## Representation and strict parser audit (2026-11-15)

`docs/element-representation-audit.md` maps the §4 concepts to the parser's existing element types. The spec's Button is handled by the unknown-element passthrough and extended renderer, avoiding a parallel core command model. During the audit, the parser's optional Boolean helper was found to coerce quoted `"true"` for `label.show` and rule `failWhenUndefined`. It now accepts JSON Boolean primitives only; a new `UiSchemaJsonTest` proves canonical true and quoted true stay distinct for both fields. The focused JVM and iOS simulator parser classes each pass 11 tests; full core JS and Wasm browser suites pass 577 each, zero failures/errors and 13 existing skips each. §S4-00 is partial because wider option and target conformance remain open.

## Structured option merge audit (2026-11-15)

`docs/structured-option-merge-policy.md` records the current merge policies for layout, dialog, pagination, row detail, cells, columns and composite actions. Dialog flags and row-detail flags now require typed Booleans; quoted `"true"`/`"false"` cannot activate them. Focused JVM and iOS simulator `DetailDialogOptionsTest` plus `ArrayRowDetailOptionsTest` pass nine cases each. Full core JS and Wasm browser suites pass 579 tests each, zero failures/errors and 13 existing skips each. `:core:compileAndroidMain` passes offline; Android runtime remains unverified. §S5.3-03 is partial pending the rest of the structured-option inventory and mounted target checks.

The follow-up added tuple and conditional-field policies. Tuple local flags require JSON Booleans and otherwise inherit typed tuple config; quoted Boolean schema slots and tails no longer become Boolean schemas. Conditional-field activation also requires typed values, a quoted schema `if` yields a diagnostic without selecting a branch, and a quoted `readOnly` facet is not promoted into the projected schema. Focused `TupleSchemaTest` and `ConditionalObjectFieldsTest` pass 19 cases on both JVM and iOS simulator. Full core JS and Wasm suites pass 582 tests each, zero failures/errors and 13 existing skips each. `:core:compileAndroidMain` passes offline. Remaining structured options and Android runtime behavior are open.

The next typed-value pass covers table required markers, dynamic label summaries, control inline-error filtering, choice autocomplete and native AG Grid pagination overrides. Quoted Boolean strings are ignored, allowing typed lower-priority values to apply where the contract defines fallback. Five focused core classes pass 23 cases on JVM and iOS simulator. Full core JS and Wasm browser suites pass 585 each, zero failures/errors and 13 existing skips each. Android main compilation passes offline. The policy document now includes these paths. Actual AG Grid rendering remains unsupported, so its native pagination test is resolver evidence only.

## Dedicated precedence and cell readonly (2026-11-15)

The cell mapper previously returned an explicit inherited `readonly=false` before invoking the shared resolver, allowing it to bypass form-wide or UI read-only. It now always invokes `isInherentlyReadonly`, whose last fallback is the inherited prop. A new `CellTest` asserts form/UI true wins and own false still applies when no earlier source sets read-only. Focused JVM and iOS simulator CellTest each report 19 cases, with zero failures/errors and three existing skips; full core JS and Wasm browser suites each pass 586 tests with zero failures/errors and 13 existing skips. Core Android main compilation passes offline. The renderer behavior catalog now states the dedicated read-only, confirmation and validator contracts; §§S5.3-05 and S15.1-01 stay partial pending remaining mutation paths and Android runtime interaction.

## Material input style namespace (2026-11-15)

Material input chrome previously read `options.variant` and top-level `config.variant` for `standard`/`filled`/`outlined`, conflicting with the portable presentation selector. It now reads `options.material.inputVariant`, then `config.jsonformsExtended.material.inputVariant`, then the outlined default. A focused Material resolver test checks local/global precedence and ignores the old reserved keys; a core tester regression confirms a namespaced style leaves ordinary string cell selection available while portable OTP still selects a specialized presentation. Full core and Material JVM suites pass 588 and 84 cases respectively, zero failures/errors (13 existing core skips). Full JS/Wasm browser suites pass 587 core and 84 Material cases per target with zero failures/errors (13 existing core skips). Focused iOS classes pass three tests each, and Material Android main compiles offline. §S23-reserved-names-01 remains partial pending other renderer namespaces and mounted style behavior.

## Portable variant capability audit (2026-11-15)

`docs/variant-contract-audit.md` records the added behavior for stepper, accordion, chips, multi-select, OTP, tuple and splitter, and separates the unsupported AG Grid request from actual grid capability. Splitter was the one selector accepting case-folded spelling; it now requires the exact JSON string `"splitter"`, matching other canonical variant selectors. A focused registry test checks both layout orientations and rejects uppercase and unrelated values. Full extended-renderer JVM/JS/Wasm suites pass 518/510/510 cases, zero failures/errors/skips. The focused iOS `ExtendedRenderersTest` passes 14 cases, zero failures/errors/skips. Extended-renderer Android main compilation passes offline; §§S5-13 and S5.2-02 remain partial pending all variant consumers and mounted target behavior.

## Unbounded row span basis (2026-11-15)

The shared span calculation now uses the algebraically equivalent percentage-of-row expression. When Compose row constraints are unbounded, Material and Cupertino use the window container width as an explicit finite row basis; wrapped and stretched rows receive that same width. With no usable window width, they keep children in authored order in a vertical fallback. A pure core test compares the expression against measured-grid math. Paired mounted scroll tests read the layout child's measured size, avoiding bounds clipped by the 320 px scroll viewport, and confirm the window-width span plus zero writes. Full core/examples-compose JVM suites pass 589/2,115 cases (13 existing core skips); full JS and Wasm browser suites pass 588/2,086 and 588/2,089 cases respectively, zero failures/errors (13 existing core skips). Focused iOS core/layout classes pass 23/56 cases, and examples-compose Android main compiles offline. §S6-14 stays partial because narrower nested scroll viewports use the window basis and Android device geometry remains unverified.

## Raw JSON authoring scope (2026-11-15)

The demo has JSON text panes with Reload/Apply, not a graphical presentation-name chooser. The plan explicitly places graphical authoring outside the initial runtime scope. A new editor regression applies canonical `chips`, `code` and `material.inputVariant` UI options, then verifies serialized UI schema round-trip and unchanged data/schema. `EditorDraftTest` passes 21 cases each on JVM and iOS simulator; full examples-compose JS and Wasm browser suites pass 2,087 and 2,090 cases, zero failures/errors/skips. §S20-tooling-identifier-01 is excluded for this raw JSON editor and must be reopened if friendly tooling identifiers are introduced.

## Group card cover in Web Component Chrome (2026-11-22)

The imported Group card's `ImageView` authors `options.width: "100%"`. Its frame now tracks 700 and 240 dp hosts in both renderer families; the 98-case Group/adornment suite passes on JVM, JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator. The standalone `scripts/test-webcomponent-image-browser.mjs` test mounts that exact imported cover in Chrome, checks the alternative text, and verifies more than 100 distinct screenshot colors within its measured accessibility bounds. It passes against both development and production Web Component bundles. The focused `DEMO_BROWSER_TARGET=wasmJs` Chrome test in `scripts/test-demo-browser-url.mjs` opens the imported Card regions category and likewise verifies decoded cover pixels. Full extended-renderers and examples-compose JVM suites pass 518 and 2,279 cases respectively. Native-device visual presentation remains unverified.

## Cupertino native date-time picker UI synchronization (2026-11-22)

A Linux CI `examples-compose:jvmTest` report failed `cupertinoNativeDateTimeCommitsByDefault` in a Compose semantics query (`SemanticsNodeInteraction.kt:178`); the excerpt did not include the queried selector. The focused test passed locally before changes and with `TZ=UTC`, so the exact CI cause is not reproduced. The test now waits for idle around opening the picker and waits until its three scrollable date/hour/minute wheels are present before choosing the hour wheel. The four-case `TemporalDateTimeActionsUiTest` and the full 2,279-case `examples-compose:jvmTest` suite pass locally with zero failures or skips on the final test file. CI should rerun to confirm the reported host-specific failure is resolved.

## Typed input adornment object fields (2026-11-22)

The §18.3a resolver previously rendered numeric `text`/`label` fields through `JsonPrimitive.content` and treated quoted `"true"` as a decorative boolean. It now requires JSON strings for object text, icon, label, i18n and tooltip fields, and a JSON Boolean for `decorative`. New pure grammar cases cover malformed values alongside valid text and icon fallbacks; paired mounted Material/Cupertino cases confirm invalid content is absent, valid content is visible, mount makes no write, and editing changes only the form value. Full JVM core/examples suites pass 599/2,281 cases with zero failures (13 existing core skips). Core JS Node/browser reports pass 598 cases each; focused core Wasm passes four. Mounted JS, Wasm and iOS simulator reports each pass the two new cases; focused core iOS simulator passes four. The first iOS mounted test link failed because the disk filled during `dsymutil`; after removing generated build outputs, the focused iOS run passed. The browser tasks initially exited without XML reports, so they were rerun with `--rerun` and selected-case XML was inspected. Android runtime and malformed-shape diagnostics remain open.

The `check-spec-coverage` and `spec-ledger-coverage` scripts pass, while `check-spec-candidates` fails because the sibling specification checkout is at `4d368072be7d3d94bba30227c9d57108d2c50029` and the Kotlin inventory is pinned to `02edfce5749b7bb35ee728db8d1805bbb0ea35d9`. That newer source adds a Group-header accessible-name requirement and strengthens decorative-icon grammar. The checkout was left untouched; the next source refresh must reclassify the changed paragraphs and cover the new Group behavior before the candidate gate can pass. `git diff --check` passes.

## Group header accessible name (2026-11-22)

The newer sibling specification revision requires the first header Label's resolved text to be the Group accessible name, including after locale changes. Material and Cupertino now derive that name through the Label renderer's translation function. Collapsible cards put it on the disclosure control; static cards put it on the Group container. Four mounted cases per target place a Button before the first Label, switch from English to Bulgarian, verify the Group label is not repeated visually, and confirm no data write. They pass on JVM, JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator with zero failures/skips. The 98-case existing Group/adornment suite passes after the change on JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator, and the full examples-compose JVM suite passes 2,285 cases. The candidate gate remains red until the pinned source inventory and requirement anchors are refreshed to the newer sibling spec revision. Dynamic aspect resolution in the Group's parent context remains a separate partial-profile gap.

## Malformed input adornment diagnostics (2026-11-22)

The shared adornment parser now mirrors the pinned object grammar: required and optional text fields are nonempty strings, `decorative` is a JSON Boolean, decorative icons cannot carry label/i18n/tooltip, and unknown object fields or strings inside arrays are invalid. A valid decorative icon and empty top-level string/array remain inert. `invalidInputAdornments` records the offending edge and optional array index. Shared renderer dispatch emits `adornment.invalid` warnings with those details and English/Bulgarian messages without blocking valid siblings or mutating form data. The existing `adornment.unsupported` diagnostic also has Bulgarian copy. Five pure grammar cases and 50 paired mounted grammar/unsupported cases pass on JVM, JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator, zero failures/skips. The core JS Node and browser suites pass 599 cases each with 13 existing skips; focused core Wasm/iOS pass five cases each. ## Cupertino tooltip theme surface (2026-11-22)

Cupertino adornment, action and Group-indicator tooltips now use a shared `CupertinoPlainTooltip` with the family's secondary background, label color, rounded shape and shadow tokens. A mounted two-case light/dark test focuses an adornment, finds its accessible explanation, captures the popup surface, and checks a solid pixel against the active Cupertino palette without a data write. The strengthened version also finds foreground pixels close to the family's label color, computes light/dark palette contrast above 7:1, and confirms a long explanation has a width no greater than a 240 dp input host without changing host bounds. Both cases pass on JVM, JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator with zero failures/skips. The existing 98-case Group/adornment suite also passes on all four targets after the shared tooltip change. The first combined browser attempt ran out of Kotlin/Wasm compiler heap while rebuilding a cold cache at 4 GB; a standalone Wasm run with `-Pkotlin.daemon.jvmargs=-Xmx6144m --max-workers=1` passed. The first iOS test wrapper used `CupertinoAppTheme`, which needs `LocalUIViewController` unavailable in the headless simulator test runner; supplying `LocalCupertinoColors` directly made the same pixel assertion pass on iOS. Pointer/hover on physical devices, narrow physical viewport popup positioning and device visuals remain unverified.

## Clear action visibility with adornments (2026-11-22)

`AdornmentClearVisibilityUiTest` exposed a transparent, clickable clear action in both renderer families when a populated field was unfocused in keyboard mode. The shared Material and Cupertino text controls now render a same-size inert spacer while clear is hidden, preserve adornment bounds when focus reveals clear, and request input focus after clearing; this also keeps Material prefix/suffix adornments visible on the emptied field. The mounted tests check hidden semantics, focused reveal, stable adornment bounds, the shared empty representation, one form write and post-clear focus/content. The pointer cases hover an unfocused field, check clear appears without shifting adornments, then leave and check clear disappears without a write. Two password cases verify prefix/suffix and native actions do not overlap, clear precedes reveal, reveal leaves all bounds stable without a write, and clear makes exactly one write while retaining focus. The six-case class passes 6/6 on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. Material places suffix before actions; Cupertino places it after actions, and both meet the spec's nonoverlap and clear/reveal order. Four older fixture classes expected touch-visible clear controls, so their relevant setups now explicitly request touch input mode. Their 38 cases pass on iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. The focused JVM classes and full 2,316-case examples-compose JVM suite pass with zero failures/skips. An initial JS run ran out of space while many orphaned Karma webpack temp directories occupied the temporary volume; clearing those outputs restored space and the Gradle browser run passed. Initially empty or externally emptied unfocused Material prefix/suffix visibility is now covered for ordinary text, passwords and string masks; pointer hover on other control variants and physical-device focus remain open.

## Empty adorned Material text fields (2026-11-22)

The value-based Material 3 text fields expanded an empty unfocused label over the prefix and suffix slots. Adorned Material text, password and string-mask fields now use the state-based overload with `isAlwaysMinimized = true`; Cupertino continues to use its separate label. The Material state is reconciled with host data after accepted, rejected and external changes without a mount write. `AdornmentEmptyValueUiTest` mounts initial and host-driven empty values in both families, then tests an accepted edit, an overlength rejected edit, a nonempty host replacement and mid-string insertion with the cursor after the inserted character. All eleven cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures/skips. The affected JVM adornment/input regression classes and full 2,337-case examples-compose JVM suite also pass with zero failures/skips. The added password cases cover empty/unfocused mount and host replacement in both families; Material also proves that output masking, reveal and raw stored value remain independent. Material string masks now insert only display literals at the mask's raw offsets through an output transformation. The ten-case `AdornmentMaskedValueUiTest` passes on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser: initial and host-driven empty values, mid-string insertion and Backspace, formatted phone-prefix storage, and eager trailing separators. The existing unadorned mask fixture remains on its original value-based path.

Material adornment tooltips retain Material 3 `PlainTooltip` styling, which maps to `inverseSurface` and `inverseOnSurface` tokens. ## Semantic input-adornment icons (2026-11-22)

The shared adornment model now retains the semantic icon identifier and decorative flag. Material and Cupertino map the four portable `information`, `account`, `schedule` and `lock` identifiers to the same glyphs used by the React/Svelte references; an unmapped nondecorative icon shows its localized label. Mapped icons expose their localized label as the accessible name. A mapped decorative icon draws with empty semantics, and an unmapped decorative icon is omitted. The imported password fixture now checks the lock glyph and its accessible “Secret” label rather than expecting visible label text.

The final six-case `InputAdornmentsTest`, three-case `InputAdornmentIconUiTest` and 98-case `GroupRegionsAndAdornmentsUiTest` pass with zero failures/skips on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser. A direct Karma run exposed webpack's inability to resolve `source-map-loader` from the repository root; the follow-on sourcemap preprocessor exception left Karma unresponsive. `examples-compose/webpack.config.d/pages.js` now resolves that loader from the generated Karma config's package location. Fresh JS browser reports for the three-case icon class and 98-case Group/adornment class have zero skips/failures. Physical-device screen-reader output, native host icon mapping, tooltip contrast and viewport positioning remain open.

## Host-provided input-adornment icons (2026-11-22)

`LocalInputAdornmentIconResolver` lets a Compose host return a `Painter` for a semantic icon identifier. The renderer uses the painter in the same input slot, keeps the localized label as its accessible name, retains keyboard tooltip access, and falls back to the portable glyph or localized text when the host returns null. Cupertino applies its label tint; Material uses the active content tint. Two mounted Material/Cupertino tests cover a known-identifier override, a custom identifier, a null fallback, accessible names, tooltip focus and zero data writes. The expanded five-case `InputAdornmentIconUiTest` passes on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero skips/failures. The first JS compilation ran out of disk before browser execution; after removing generated Wasm caches and unrelated JS test packages, the focused browser run passed. Actual host asset appearance and physical-device accessibility remain open.

## Interpolated Group header accessible name (2026-11-22)

Material and Cupertino previously derived a Group's accessible name from the first header Label's translated source text. The extended Label renderer could display a different interpolated result after reading form data, host context and locale. A scoped `LocalGroupHeaderLabelText` now lets that first rendered Label publish its resolved text to the Group, which uses it for the collapsible disclosure or static card name. The sink is cleared when the Label leaves composition. Four mounted cases place an interactive Button before the Label, replace data from Ada to Mila, switch English to Bulgarian, and check visible heading, matching accessible Group name and zero form writes. Two further cases keep the first authored Label's interpolation available for the Group name while its own rule hides visual text, then replace the UI schema with the two header Labels reordered and verify the name changes. Together with four translation cases, all ten `GroupAccessibleNameUiTest` cases pass on JVM, iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero skips/failures. The 98-case `GroupRegionsAndAdornmentsUiTest` passes on JVM after the initial change, and the 36-case extended `LabelInterpolationUiTest` JVM suite passes after the hidden-Label change. Two further mounted cases per target use an actual array-detail Group and `{item.name}`: switching rows, replacing the selected row's name, changing to Bulgarian and returning to the first row update the Group name without writes. The eight-case `GroupArrayItemContextUiTest` passes on all four targets with zero skips/failures. Physical assistive-technology output and Android runtime remain open.

## Web Component connection and DOM island release probe (2026-11-22)

The installed Compose UI 1.12.2 JS source provides `HtmlElementView` with `onRelease`, positioned HTML overlays and clipping. The Web Component now sets a connection state on custom-element attach/detach and leaves its retained viewport's form composition empty while disconnected. Reconnecting refreshes attributes and reuses the same viewport. The 12-case Node registration suite passes; the rebuilt development and production bundles each pass the mounted Chrome host scenario, including an edited-field detach/reinsert with retained data. Gradle rejected a combined development/production webpack invocation because of an implicit output dependency, so both build tasks were run separately and passed.

A new `webcomponent:jsBrowserTest` Chrome case mounts a 100 dp HTML button through `HtmlElementView` inside a clipped 50 dp Compose parent, verifies DOM presence, size, generated clip-path style and focus, removes its Compose branch, checks DOM removal and exactly one `onRelease` callback, and remounts it. The final one-case report has zero failures or skips. The first two attempts failed at Skiko's GL initialization in the default Karma launcher; the test now uses a WebGL-capable Chrome launcher and a Skiko readiness hook. A deliberate temporary wrong release-count assertion failed as expected, proving the browser runner awaits the Promise assertions; that assertion was restored before the final passing run. This proves a basic conditional DOM island release path and clipping style, not scrolling, visual clipping, original-path child delegation, disconnection-triggered engine cleanup or disposal of the Compose/WebGL viewport itself.

## Supplied Slot precedence correction (2026-11-22)

The pinned §13.4 text says a later same-name supplied child replaces an earlier child and inherited content; unnamed children do not fill a Slot. The previous shared merge incorrectly diagnosed duplicate supplied names and rendered the fallback. It now uses the last supplied name and leaves duplicate-name diagnostics for ambiguous Template registry lookup. The paired Material/Cupertino mounted case checks the winner, excludes the unnamed child and fallback, reverses supplied order live, and confirms the visible winner switches without a diagnostic or data write. The 21-case Template/Slot class and the focused shared renderer class pass on JVM and JS browser with zero failures/skips. The full workflow JVM step passes under UTC with 522 extended-renderer, 20 examples and 2,383 examples-compose tests, zero failures/errors/skips; both Kotlin/JS compile tasks and production browser distributions pass. Wasm/iOS/Android and nested-template inheritance remain open.

## Delegated ShadowRoot DOM island proof (2026-11-22)

A second `DomIslandInteropJsTest` case mounts a high-ranked Control renderer through the ordinary JSON Forms registry in a `ComposeViewport` hosted inside a ShadowRoot. Its HTML button exposes the mapped `name` data path, starts within the Compose scroll viewport, moves with a 30 px scroll, and takes focus in the ShadowRoot. Form-gated host removal calls `onRelease` once and removes the button; reconnecting the same host remounts the button, and a second removal calls `onRelease` once more. No form data writes occur. Both DOM-island cases pass in Chrome with zero failures or skips. The test uses a probe renderer and an equivalent connection gate; production Web Component registry wiring, real engine resource cleanup and viewport disposal remain open.

## Nested Template/Slot caller path (2026-11-22)

Paired mounted Material/Cupertino cases place a named Template in an authored object detail at `person`, supply a named Slot Control, and edit `person.name`. The write leaves sibling `other.name` unchanged; a later external replacement updates the visible nested value without another write. Only the host error at `/person/name` appears in the nested Control, and its DISABLE rule follows `person.locked` rather than `other.locked`. The rule assertion first exposed that standard schema-based conditions were ignoring the nested rendering path. `RuleEvaluator` now resolves those scopes in the current data context while direct pointer and host function-condition scoped data keep their existing root behavior; a complete core run caught and protected the latter contract. The 23-case Template/Slot class passes on JVM and JS browser with zero failures/skips. Core JVM passes 602 tests with 13 existing skips; the full workflow JVM step passes under UTC with 524 extended-renderer, 20 examples and 2,383 examples-compose tests, zero failures/errors/skips. Focused core JS Node and Template/Slot Chrome tests, both Kotlin/JS compile tasks and both production browser distributions pass. Wasm/iOS/Android runtime remains open.

## Unset cron period and color fixture timing (2026-11-23)

The pinned cron contract asks for a period first, but the Kotlin popup previously preselected Every minute for absent or unparseable values. It now shows the localized Choose how often prompt, hides field selectors until a period is selected, and keeps the original value when a period is chosen without a field edit. Choosing hour 9 from an empty or invalid value produces `0 0 9 * * *`. Six new Material/Cupertino regressions cover absent English/Bulgarian values and invalid raw text; the 34-case cron class passes on JVM, Chrome JS browser, Chrome Wasm browser and arm64 iOS simulator with zero failures/errors/skips.

An initial broad JS run completed 2,426 tests with two 2-second timeouts in the existing six-interaction color fixture cases. The first filtered retry selected no runnable cases and reported one skipped placeholder, so it was not counted as a pass. A method-matching retry reproduced one timeout. The fixture now gives each authored storage mode its own test while retaining the same assertions. Its 14-case class passes on JS browser, Wasm browser and iOS simulator. The full JS suite then passed 2,436 tests with zero failures/errors/skips; this full run preceded the final two cron-only invalid-value cases, which passed separately in the 34-case focused class on all four targets. The full seven-module JVM gate passes 602 core, 19 Compose, 84 Material, six Cupertino, 536 extended-renderer, 20 examples and 2,459 examples-compose tests with zero failures/errors and 13 existing core skips. The Pages JS demo, Wasm demo and Web Component production distributions pass, with their entry pages observed. ## Cupertino date-time time-step parity (2026-11-23)

The default Cupertino date-time control previously used a combined date/hour/minute wheel popup while standalone Time used `CupertinoTimePickerDialog`. It now opens the native date picker first, advances with Next, and uses that same Time picker for the time step; Material already used its standalone time picker. The date stage rejects days with no selectable minute under restricted schema bounds, and the time stage retains unshown seconds. The obsolete combined dialog wrapper was removed. ## Temporal placeholder draft preservation (2026-11-24)

Six mounted Material/Cupertino date, time and date-time regressions first failed because a placeholder-only UI-schema replacement cleared an invalid focused draft and changed Pending validity to Valid. Draft and picker state now reset on path, schema or authored option changes other than `placeholder`; a hint-only update retains the draft, focus and pending status without a form write. Renderer-selection regressions change `dateFormat` and `mask` to verify that incompatible drafts still reset when the input contract changes. The temporal string option reader also rejects numeric and Boolean JSON primitives as placeholders and format strings.

Full unfiltered examples-compose browser suites pass 2,634 JS and 2,633 Wasm cases with no failures or skips. The exact JS, Wasm and Web Component Pages distribution tasks and `:examples-compose-android:assembleDebug` pass on this revision. Physical-device IME and accessibility output, plus other hint-bearing controls, remain open.

## Native fallback placeholder conformance (2026-11-24)

Material and Cupertino native date/time fallback controls now use an authored string placeholder ahead of their format hint. An explicit empty string suppresses that hint; a numeric placeholder is ignored and returns to the format hint. The paired mounted regression failed in both families before the change and now passes without a form data write. It runs through the actual native fallback registry entries on JVM, arm64 iOS simulator, JS browser and Wasm browser with zero failures or skips. The existing Cupertino invalid-import fallback cases also pass on all four targets.

The full unfiltered examples-compose browser suites pass 2,636 JS and 2,635 Wasm cases with no failures or skips. The seven-module JVM gate passes core 607 (13 existing skips), Compose 21, Material 90, Cupertino 6, extended renderers 547, examples 20 and examples-compose 2,653 with zero failures or errors. The exact JS, Wasm and Web Component Pages distributions and Android debug APK build pass. Dynamic placeholders, other hint-bearing controls and physical-device accessibility remain open.

## Mask token defaults and commit gate (2026-11-24)

The shared mask now recognizes default ASCII `#`, `@` and `*` tokens, custom token objects with a `pattern`, and default-token removal or restoration through `tokensReplace`. Invalid regexes and malformed truthy token entries leave an existing default intact; falsy entries remove it. Seven pure mask cases pass on JVM, JS browser and Wasm browser. Paired Material/Cupertino mounted tests cover the new default and object-token paths on JVM. The exact Pages JVM test, browser compilation and all three distribution tasks pass on the corrected worktree. The full unfiltered examples-compose browser suites pass 2,650 JS and 2,649 Wasm tests with zero failures, errors or skips. `git diff --check` passes. Earlier browser stalls came from stale Karma webpack temp directories exhausting local disk space; those generated directories were cleared before the successful reruns.

## Ancient Material date picker follow-up (2026-11-24)

A typed `03/12/1220` persisted correctly but the picker initially fell back to the current month because Compose Material3's default year range is 1900–2100. Extending the range exposed three formatter differences: the desktop month heading used a historical Julian date, the browser formatted UTC midnight in local time, and the iOS selected-day description showed a historical calendar offset. `MaterialDatePickerDialog` now uses years 1–9999, English/Bulgarian Gregorian labels for pre-1583 dates, and a same-day formatting instant for later dates. `TemporalAncientDatePickerUiTest` has four mounted cases (ancient date, ancient date-time, modern date, Bulgarian ancient date) with stored-value, month-heading and selected-day assertions. The exact `:examples-compose:jvmTest`, `:examples-compose:jsBrowserTest`, `:examples-compose:wasmJsBrowserTest` and `:examples-compose:iosSimulatorArm64Test` focused class runs each report four tests, zero failures/errors. Focused `:material-renderers:jvmTest`, `:material-renderers:jsBrowserTest`, `:material-renderers:wasmJsBrowserTest` and `:material-renderers:iosSimulatorArm64Test` runs with `--tests '*TemporalFormatsTest*'` pass, including English/Bulgarian historical labels and February 29 conversion. The existing `TemporalDateTimeActionsUiTest` and imported `TemporalControlsFixtureUiTest` also pass on JVM. `:examples-compose-android:assembleDebug --offline --no-configuration-cache` succeeds; Android interaction was not run. Cupertino ancient date and date-time wheels subsequently pass focused center-selection tests on JVM/iOS and the browser class on JS/Wasm (two new cases, zero failures); Cupertino's year range now clamps to 1–9999; two mounted boundary tests exclude years 0 and 10000 on JVM/iOS, while the full eight-case ancient-date class passes on JS/Wasm. The full `:examples-compose:jvmTest` task reports 2,677 tests with zero failures/errors, and `:examples-compose-android:assembleDebug` succeeds after the Cupertino range change. Material endpoint tests also select January 2 in years 1 and 9999 on JVM/iOS; the complete ten-case ancient-date class passes on JS/Wasm. The exact GitHub Pages task set (`:extended-renderers:jvmTest`, `:examples:jvmTest`, `:examples-compose:jvmTest`, JS/Wasm/Web Component compilation and all three production browser distributions) passes locally: 547/20/2,677 JVM tests, zero failures/errors. Android debug also builds. Other picker bounds and physical-device accessibility remain unverified.

No inventory refresh was applied to this Kotlin change.
